14:T1977,## Summary
The route-level model downloader `downloadModel` in `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/local-inference-routes.ts` resumes an interrupted download by sending a `Range: bytes=<n>-` header and then opening the staging file in append mode whenever a `.part` file exists (`flags: existingPartial > 0 ? "a" : "w"`). It never verifies the server actually honored the range (HTTP 206). If the origin ignores Range and replies HTTP 200 with the full body — common for CDN redirect targets, gated/proxy repos, or mirrors — the full file is appended onto the stale partial bytes, producing a corrupt GGUF. `record.total` is also miscomputed as `existingPartial + fullSize`. The corruption passes the only integrity check (`isGgufFile` reads just the first 4 bytes, which are still "GGUF" from the old partial), and there is no sha256 verification against an expected hash, so the corrupt file is renamed to final, registered as an installed model, and auto-assigned to the TEXT_SMALL/TEXT_LARGE/TEXT_EMBEDDING slots via `ensureDefaultAssignment`. The sibling canonical `services/downloader.ts` in the same package already guards this exact case (`if (startByte > 0 && response.statusCode !== 206) { startByte = 0; received = 0 }` then `flags: startByte > 0 ? "a" : "w"`); the route-level copy diverged and dropped the guard.

## Reproduction
```
Ran a local Vitest probe in plugins/plugin-local-inference (real fs + real local HTTP origin; only ELIZA_STATE_DIR and ELIZA_HF_BASE_URL redirected). Steps: (1) set ELIZA_HF_BASE_URL to a local http server that IGNORES Range and always answers `200` with the full body `GGUF`+`REAL-MODEL-BODY` (19 bytes); (2) seed a stale partial `<state>/local-inference/downloads/eliza-1-2b.part` = `GGUF`+`STALE-PARTIAL`; (3) call the exported `applyLocalInferenceManagementMutation({ op: "start_download", modelId: "eliza-1-2b" })`, which runs `startDownload` -> `downloadModel`; (4) poll for the final file `<state>/local-inference/models/eliza-1-2b.gguf`.
Observed output: `stored length: 36 expected: 19` and `stored bytes: "GGUFSTALE-PARTIALGGUFREAL-MODEL-BODY"`. Expected: exactly `GGUFREAL-MODEL-BODY` (19 bytes). The corrupt file was renamed to final and written into registry.json as an installed model. Test asserted `stored.equals(FULL)` -> failed (false). Command: `npx vitest run src/__probe_resume.test.ts`.
```

## Proposed fix
Mirror the sibling downloader's guard in `downloadModel`: after receiving the response and validating 2xx, compute `let effectiveStart = existingPartial; if (existingPartial > 0 && response.statusCode !== 206) { effectiveStart = 0; record.received = 0; }`, then use `effectiveStart` for both `record.total = effectiveStart + contentLength` and `flags: effectiveStart > 0 ? "a" : "w"`. Bounded to ~6 lines in one function; no signature/API change. Test plan (>=10 lines, honest): a Vitest suite standing up two local http origins — one that honors Range (206 + remaining bytes) and one that ignores it (200 + full body) — seeding a stale `.part`, driving `applyLocalInferenceManagementMutation({op:"start_download"})`, and asserting the final `models/<id>.gguf` bytes exactly equal the full file in BOTH cases (correct resume for 206, clean restart for 200); plus a regression assertion that `record.total`/registry size match the true file length. The probe already written proves the failing case and converts directly into the 200-path test.

## Evidence plan
The PR will attach: the failing-before / passing-after Vitest output for both the 200 (ignore-range) and 206 (honor-range) origins; a hexdump/length comparison of the stored `models/<id>.gguf` showing 19 bytes (correct) vs the current 36-byte `GGUFSTALE-PARTIALGGUFREAL-MODEL-BODY` corruption; and a diff showing the route-level guard now matches services/downloader.ts (statusCode !== 206 -> restart from 0).

## Duplicate search
gh search issues/prs --repo elizaOS/eliza --state all for: "local-inference resume download", "local-inference partial download corrupt", "download Range 206 resume", "downloadModel append partial", "local-inference download", "resume download corrupt", "model download partial" — all returned zero results. grep for an existing 206 guard in plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/local-inference-routes.ts found none (only the sibling services/downloader.ts and its test reference 206).

_Filed under a standing operator authorization for the autonomous audit pipeline, after local reproduction and duplicate search._

## Consolidated download-integrity scope — 2026-09-14

This issue is the canonical owner for duplicate #29454 and the related Content-Range integrity requirement from #30939. Preserve both route-level and canonical downloader consumers, including the UI host copy, in the implementation review. #29456 is retained as the narrow 200-response restart candidate; it is not complete proof of the full contract. #30888 and #30940 are unsuitable implementations for the reasons recorded on those PRs.

Acceptance: append only when a valid 206 Content-Range matches the exact local offset; a full 200 response restarts from byte zero. Missing, malformed or mismatched ranges must not be treated as proof that append is safe. If the returned body starts at a nonzero offset that cannot be placed, reject or refetch the complete entity before replacing the partial; never write a tail fragment at offset zero. Validate start/end/total consistency and safe integer bounds. Exercise real temporary files and an HTTP origin covering ignored Range, correct resume, wrong-offset response, missing/malformed range, cancellation and retry. Assert final bytes and preservation of recoverable state, not only status codes or file magic.

Use one shared protocol decision where package dependency boundaries allow it; do not clone divergent range parsers into each downloader. Existing candidate work and reproduction history remain linked in the source issues. Closure of those leaves means consolidated tracking, not repaired downloads.
15:T5f3,## Problem

Review of the Android speech implementation merged by #30759 at `63323d0f77f2b48eb6221fafbcb243ab3154b21d` found that coroutine cancellation can enter provider-failure handlers, the bionic inference socket is not closed by stop/teardown, and overlapping speech requests share playback resources without waiting for prior cleanup.

The affected consumer is the Android TalkMode `speak`/`stopSpeaking` bridge. Interrupting or destroying a session during local inference can leave a blocking request alive, report a phonemization failure instead of interruption, or start fallback speech. A replacement request can also lose its resources to an older request's cleanup.

## Acceptance

- Preserve coroutine cancellation through phonemization and provider fallback handlers; settle the bridge result as interrupted.
- Close both local-agent and bionic inference sockets on stop and teardown, including connection/read phases.
- Serialize playback resource ownership so a cancelled request finishes cleanup before its replacement starts; cancelled queued calls must also settle.
- Exercise the actual Kotlin implementation for cancellation and replacement ordering, compile the Android plugin, and run the owning package/repository checks.
- Verify stop, immediate replacement, and teardown on the installed current Android revision, including no fallback playback after cancellation and successful subsequent speech.

The isolated implementation is in progress. No passing device evidence is claimed by this issue.
16:Te53,The installed Android local agent cannot initialize the Notes service on a fresh agent-scoped store. `NotesStore.writeAtomicIfAbsent` publishes its fsynced temporary file with `fs.link`; Android denies that hard link with `EACCES`. The service reports `NOTES_STORE_WRITE_FAILED` and never becomes ready.

Reproduced on emulator-5580 with the current combined Android build fd755929592ee25f5268853015d3d5ee1cac450c. An independent invocation of the production NotesStore through the APK's installed Bun, using a disposable directory under the app's files directory, fails with the same nested `EACCES` / `link` cause. The disposable directory was removed; existing notes and app data were preserved.

Acceptance:
- Fresh Android Notes initialization succeeds through the installed runtime without hard-link permission.
- Publication remains atomic and must never overwrite a document created by a concurrent initializer.
- Existing state remains readable and mutable; malformed state fails explicitly.
- Interrupted publication cannot expose an empty or partial valid-looking document.
- Exercise real installed Android CRUD/restart and concurrent initialization, plus the package tests, typecheck, lint and root verify.

Current implementation and validation:
- Repaired production NotesStore uses an atomic nonempty-directory publication when the filesystem denies hard links; existing flat documents remain authoritative.
- All 119 Notes package tests, typecheck, lint and guide parity pass. Four new real-filesystem tests cover restart mutations/deletion, preserving a competing initializer's notes, incomplete publication, and legacy flat state.
- On Android emulator-5580, four separate installed-Bun processes initialized one fresh store concurrently. Five subsequent fresh processes created, edited, read, deleted, and verified the deletion. All passed without a filesystem mock. The disposable test directory was removed.
- This device proof ran the repaired production store as a separate bundle through the installed runtime. Rebuilding the APK with the repaired Notes service and final combined repository gates remain pending; this issue is not yet closed.

Downloadable evidence (uploaded assets were read back and SHA-256 verified):
- [119 package tests](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-10/30725-package-tests.log)
- [Focused publication regressions](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-10/30725-publication-tests.log)
- [Android concurrent initialization and fresh-process persistence](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-10/30725-device-store-proof.json)

17:T81b,## Summary

plugin-computeruse's OCR / Set-of-Marks provider registry is bundled twice, so plugin-vision's registration never reaches the readers in packaged builds. `plugins/plugin-computeruse/build.ts:52` builds `src/mobile/ocr-provider.ts` as a standalone entrypoint (`dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js`) while `src/index.ts` also inlines the same module into `dist/index.js` (there the writer is tree-shaken and only `var registeredCoordOcrProvider = null` plus the getter survive). plugin-vision writes through `await import("@elizaos/plugin-computeruse/mobile/ocr-provider")` (`plugins/plugin-vision/src/index.ts:118-141`), which resolves to the subpath bundle; the readers `computer-use-service.ts:1231` (`runOcrOrDetect`), `scene/ocr-adapter.ts:98,156` and `app-control/defaults.ts:88,114` live in `dist/index.js` and read the other module instance.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`, built dist)

```
$ grep -c 'function registerCoordOcrProvider' dist/index.js dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js
dist/index.js:0
dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js:1
```

A probe importing both bundles without `--conditions`, registering through the subpath and listing through the index: `subpath listOcrProviders -> ["probe-ocr"]` / `index listOcrProviders -> []`.

## Consequence

In packaged desktop or npm-consumed builds `ocr` and `detect_elements` always fail with "No OCR provider is registered. Enable @elizaos/plugin-vision…" and `scene.ocr` stays empty even with plugin-vision loaded, while vision logs "registered coord-OCR bridge". Every repo dev/start script passes `--conditions=eliza-source` (both imports resolve to `src/`), which masks it. `plugins/plugin-computeruse/CLAUDE.md:275` documents this registry as the extension path.

## Acceptance criteria

- The index bundle and the `mobile/ocr-provider` subpath share one module instance (mark the subpath external in the index build, or have the index re-export from the built subpath), and a test proves a provider registered through the published subpath is visible to `runOcrOrDetect` without `--conditions=eliza-source`.
18:T706,## Summary

Meeting-ghost relative due dates are resolved on the UTC calendar day of the meeting. `parseDueDate` in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/meeting-ghost/index.ts:355-370` does `new Date(meetingStartedAt)`, adds days with `setUTCDate`, reads the weekday with `getUTCDay()` and formats with `toISOString().slice(0,10)`; `dueDateToLedgerDueAt` (373-377) then pins `${dueDate}T17:00:00.000Z` and the calendar deadline (436) `T09:00:00.000Z`. `event-handler.ts:67` feeds every transcript through this path.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

Probe calling the exported `analyzeMeetingGhostTranscript` (results are offset-driven, identical under any process TZ):

```
startedAt=2026-09-14T17:30:00-07:00 "Alice will send the report by tomorrow" -> dueDate=2026-09-16  (Mon 5:30pm PT; tomorrow is 09-15)
startedAt=2026-09-17T17:30:00-07:00 "Bob will send the deck by Friday"       -> dueDate=2026-09-25  (Thu 5:30pm PT; Friday is 09-18)
startedAt=2026-09-14T08:00:00+09:00 "Alice will send the report by tomorrow" -> dueDate=2026-09-14  (Mon 8am JST; tomorrow is 09-15)
startedAt=2026-09-14T08:00:00+09:00 "Carol will file the taxes by Monday"    -> dueDate=2026-09-14  (said on Monday; next Monday is 09-21)
```

## Consequence

The commitment ledger `dueAt`, the follow-up approvals and the "Deadline:" calendar intent carry the wrong day, a full week off in the weekday case, for any meeting whose local evening is already the next UTC day (afternoon/evening in the Americas) or whose local morning is still the previous UTC day (east of UTC).

## Acceptance criteria

- Relative dates are resolved on the meeting's local calendar day (the transcript's or runtime's timezone), and the ledger/calendar instants are built from that zone.
- Tests pin the four cases above.
19:Tf99,### What happens

`validateActionParams` in `packages/core/src/actions.ts` (the version at the time of writing is reproduced below) returns `valid: true` with an **empty `errors` array** when an **optional** parameter is supplied with a value that fails its own declared schema — as long as that parameter declares no `schema.default`. The offending value is silently removed from the returned `params`.

The caller therefore cannot distinguish *"the model did not supply this parameter"* from *"the model supplied an invalid value that was discarded"*, and the action handler runs with a **different input than the one requested**, while still reporting success.

### Minimal reproduction

Uses only the public API. Run against `@elizaos/core@2.0.3-beta.7` (Node 26):

```js
import { validateActionParams } from '@elizaos/core';

const action = {
  name: 'DEMO',
  description: 'demo action',
  validate: async () => true,
  handler: async () => ({ success: true }),
  parameters: [
    { name: 'range', description: 'a bounded optional number', required: false,
      schema: { type: 'number', minimum: 0, maximum: 100 } },
    { name: 'token', description: 'required', required: true,
      schema: { type: 'string', pattern: '^0x[0-9a-fA-F]{40}$' } },
  ],
};

show('in range       ', { range: 50,  token: '0x' + 'a'.repeat(40) });
show('out of range   ', { range: 500, token: '0x' + 'a'.repeat(40) });
show('required missing', { range: 50 });
show('required invalid', { range: 50, token: 'bad' });
```

Observed output (verbatim):

```
in range       
   -> {"valid":true,"params":{"range":50,"token":"0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"},"errors":[]}
out of range   
   -> {"valid":true,"params":{"token":"0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"},"errors":[]}
required missing
   -> {"valid":false,"params":{"range":50},"errors":["Required parameter 'token' was not provided for action DEMO"]}
required invalid
   -> {"valid":false,"params":{"range":50},"errors":["Parameter 'token' value 'bad' does not match pattern ^0x[0-9a-fA-F]{40}$"]}
```

Note the second line: `range: 500` violates `maximum: 100`, yet the result is `valid: true` with `errors: []` and the value silently gone. The two **required**-parameter violations below it *are* reported, so the reporting path clearly exists — the optional branch just does not use it.

### Why this is worth fixing

- An action that bounds or filters its work from an optional parameter can **silently run unbounded**, returning a plausible-looking result for a request that was not honoured. In our case it produced a result computed without the filter the caller asked for.
- **`HandlerOptions.parameterErrors` cannot be used as a workaround.** It is populated from this same `errors` array, which is empty here — so a handler has no signal that anything was dropped, and cannot detect it even after the fact.
- Two further asymmetries are easy to miss while reading the code:
  - `schema.type === 'number'` is required for `minimum`/`maximum` to be applied at all; with `type: 'integer'` they are ignored entirely.
  - Declaring a `schema.default` changes the outcome from *dropped* to *silently replaced by the default* — also invisible to the caller.

### Possible directions (maintainer's call)

1. Record an error for an optional parameter whose **supplied** value fails validation, distinct from "not provided", so it reaches `parameterErrors` and the handler can react; or
2. surface the rejected parameter name/value so the handler can decide; or
3. if the current behaviour is intended, document it explicitly — right now the contract reads as "invalid input is reported", and the optional case breaks that.

Happy to open a PR if a direction is preferred — and to test whatever shape is chosen against a real runtime.

*(Found while building a plugin that declares bounded optional parameters, verified against a real `AgentRuntime` before filing. No other part of this report depends on that plugin.)*

1d:Tc39,## Summary

Generated media that arrives as a `data:` URL (Google Imagen images, TTS audio, voice-first audio) is undeliverable on Discord and Telegram. Discord's SDK resolves the string as a filesystem path (ENOENT, caption lost with it); Telegram's `sendMedia` throws `File not found at path: data:image/png;base64,<whole blob>`, aborts the reply before the text is sent, and logs the full base64 payload twice.

## Where (develop `c2bb8860be`)

- Producers: `packages/agent/src/services/media-generation.ts:98-103` `bytesToAudioDataUrl` → `data:audio/mpeg;base64,…` (used at `:201` for TTS); `packages/core/src/features/advanced-capabilities/actions/generateMedia.ts:227` `data:image/png;base64,${result.imageBase64}` for base64-only image providers; `packages/core/src/services/message/delivery.ts:155` voice-first `data:audio/wav;base64,…`.
- The media-persist hook that would rewrite these into `/api/media/<sha>.<ext>` handles (`packages/agent/src/api/media-runtime.ts:127-151`, phase `outgoing_before_deliver`) runs from `processor.ts:713/1303/1500` and the autonomy execution facade; the action callback that delivers `GENERATE_MEDIA` output reaches the connector send handlers without passing through it.
- Discord: `plugins/plugin-discord/utils.ts:339-341` wraps the string in `AttachmentBuilder("data:…")`; for audio/video the guarded fetch rejects non-http and the catch at `:372-377` falls through to the same `AttachmentBuilder("data:…")`; discord.js 14.27.0 `DataResolver.resolveFile` then does `path.resolve` + `fs.stat` → ENOENT, and because files ride on the last text chunk (`utils.ts:690-700`) the caption in the same `channel.send` is lost too.
- Telegram: `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:1121-1160` sends attachments in a `Promise.all` before the text; `:1425-1428` throws `File not found at path: ${mediaPath}` with the whole data URL; `:1464-1476` `logger.error({ mediaPath, error: error.message })` logs the blob twice; the rethrow aborts `sendMessageInChunks`, and the turn's `delivery_started` marker (`:1575-1610`) refuses a replay.
- Slack: `plugins/plugin-slack/src/service.ts:2776-2793` rejects via the fetch guard and logs `{ url: media.url }`, i.e. the blob.

## Input → wrong output

`GENERATE_MEDIA` with the Google image provider (base64-only) or any TTS request → on Discord the caption and image are both lost (ENOENT); on Telegram nothing reaches the user, the log carries megabytes of base64, and the turn cannot be retried. Providers that return https URLs (OpenAI) work, which matches the live Discord evidence in PR #20460.

## Fix direction

Run the media-persist hook on action-callback deliveries too (so `data:` becomes a canonical media-store handle) and make Discord and Telegram resolve store handles to bytes; or decode `data:` URLs into a Buffer in `buildOutboundDiscordAttachment` and Telegram `sendMedia` (`{ source: buffer }` for Telegraf). Never put a `data:` payload into an error message or a log field (log a hash or length instead). Telegram should send text independently of attachments so one bad attachment cannot suppress the reply.

## Fix direction

1f:T77a,## Summary

`persistMediaBytes` in `packages/agent/src/api/media-store.ts` (~400-406) writes `<sha256>.<ext>` with `fs.writeFileSync` directly to its final name and skips the write whenever a file with that name already exists. `writeFileSync` truncates and writes in chunks, so an `ENOSPC` or a crash mid-write leaves a truncated file under the correct content hash. Every later persist of the same bytes (a retry after freeing disk, the same image attached again, a backup restore) sees the file exists, skips the write, and returns a success URL that serves the truncated bytes with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`. Nothing repairs it: GC and eviction only consider mtime and references. The same pattern is in `writeStoredMediaFile` (restore path), `persistPrivateMediaBytes` and the pins ledger write.

## Reproduction (develop `a6863aa318`, real module in a temporary `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`)

```
pre-seeded 100-byte file under the sha256 of a 4096-byte payload
persistMediaBytes(payload) -> url=/api/media/<hash>.bin, file size on disk 100 (expected 4096)

on a 64 KiB tmpfs with a 200 KiB payload:
first persist -> ENOSPC, partial file exists (65536 bytes)
second persist -> success URL; route serves 200 with 65536 bytes and the immutable cache header
```

## Impact

One disk-full event permanently corrupts specific attachments in a user's history, and content addressing guarantees the corruption is reused for every future identical upload and cached forever by the browser.

## Fix direction

Write to a temporary sibling (`<name>.<pid>.<random>.tmp`), fsync, then rename over the final name, removing the temporary file on any failure (the auth package's account file writer already does this); treat an existing file whose size differs from the payload as absent and rewrite it. Apply to all four write sites and pin with a test that pre-seeds a short file under the correct hash.

23:T891,## Summary

The Twilio and Blooio inbound webhooks use check-then-mark idempotency: `packages/cloud/api/webhooks/twilio/[orgId]/route.ts` calls `isAlreadyProcessed(idempotencyKey)` (~line 147), then `handleIncomingMessage` (~166), then `markAsProcessed` (~167); `webhooks/blooio/[orgId]/route.ts` has the same shape (~107-112, ~168). `handleIncomingMessage` runs agent inference, sends the reply through `messageRouterService.sendMessage`, and records a `twilio_sms` usage row keyed by `MessageSid`, so it routinely exceeds Twilio's 15 s webhook timeout. Any second delivery of the same `MessageSid` / message id that arrives before the first finishes (a fallback URL pointed at the same endpoint, an operator replay, a duplicate POST with a valid signature, or a non-production deployment with `SKIP_WEBHOOK_VERIFICATION=true`) passes the check and is processed in full again: two outbound SMS (real provider spend), two inference runs, two usage rows. `isAlreadyProcessed` also fails open on a DB error. The atomic primitive already exists and is what the WhatsApp webhook uses: `tryClaimForProcessing` (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) with `releaseProcessingClaim` on failure (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/utils/idempotency.ts`, `webhooks/whatsapp/[orgId]/route.ts:110-133`).

## Reproduction (develop `1eba34ba95`, real primitives on PGlite with `idempotency_keys` pushed)

Two concurrent Twilio-style sequences (`isAlreadyProcessed` -> 50 ms work -> `markAsProcessed`) on key `twilio:SM_probe`, versus two concurrent `tryClaimForProcessing("whatsapp:probe")`:

```
check-then-mark results [ "processed", "processed" ]  handled 2
tryClaim results        [ true, false ]
```

The route-level consequence follows from the code order above; the full route was not stood up (it needs an org, a phone gateway and a routed agent).

## Fix direction

Claim before processing in both routes exactly as WhatsApp does: `tryClaimForProcessing(key, "twilio")`; on `false` return the empty TwiML 200; on handler failure `releaseProcessingClaim(key)` so a genuine retry can proceed. Open PR #27661 touches `tryClaimForProcessing` (expired-key reclaim) but does not move these two routes onto it.

27:Tef0,**Describe the bug**

Two catch blocks in `plugins/plugin-wallet/src/chains/solana/service.ts` (develop `cbf357b0bd8a`) turn an RPC failure into a healthy-looking zero, and three consumers persist or serve that zero as fact.

1. `getTokenAccountsByKeypair` (lines 2104-2107) returns `[]` from its catch. `updateWalletData` (lines 1975-1986) treats `accounts.length === 0` as an empty portfolio and writes `{ totalUsd: "0", totalSol: "0", items: [] }` to `SOLANA_WALLET_DATA_CACHE_KEY`. The Solana wallet provider (`providers/wallet.ts`, lines 66-71) renders that cache into the planner prompt as `Total Value: $0.00 (0 SOL)` and `No tokens found with non-zero balance`, and `GET /wallet/balance/:token` (`routes/index.ts`, line 119) answers `404 TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` for a token the wallet holds.
2. `getBalancesByAddrs` (lines 2145-2154) returns `{}` from its catch; `GET /wallet/balance` (`routes/index.ts`, line 73) applies `balances[publicKeyStr] ?? 0` and answers `200` with `balance: 0`. The same catch retries on a `429` message with `return this.getBalancesByAddrs(...)` and no attempt counter, so a sustained rate limit is an unbounded recursion and the request never returns.

Both routes have their own `catch` that would have produced a correct 500; the service swallows the error first. This is the pattern the repository error policy forbids (a default return from a catch, and `?? <literal>` disguising missing required data).

**To Reproduce**

Real `SolanaService`, real routes and real provider; only the RPC `Connection` is replaced by a stub that answers HTTP 500 to every call:

```
--- service layer ---
getBalancesByAddrs  -> {}
 Error      Error fetching token accounts: Error: failed to get info about accounts: Server responded with 500 Internal Server Error
getTokenAccountsByKeypair -> []
--- HTTP routes (RPC is returning 500 for every call) ---
GET /wallet/balance -> HTTP 200 {"success":true,"data":{"publicKey":"11111111111111111111111111111112","balance":0,"symbol":"SOL"}}
GET /wallet/portfolio -> HTTP 200 {"success":true,"data":{"publicKey":"1111...1112","totalUsd":"0","totalSol":"0","tokens":[],"hasBirdeyeData":false}}
GET /wallet/balance/:token (USDC) -> HTTP 404 {"success":false,"error":{"code":"TOKEN_NOT_FOUND","message":"Token EPjFWdd5AufqSSqeM2qN1xzybapC8G4wEGGkZwyTDt1v not found in wallet"}}
--- portfolio cache written while RPC is down ---
updateWalletData() -> {"totalUsd":"0","totalSol":"0","items":[]}
cached under SOLANA_WALLET_DATA_CACHE_KEY: [["solana/walletData",{"totalUsd":"0","totalSol":"0","items":[]}]]
provider text injected into the planner prompt:
Eliza's Main Solana Wallet (11111111111111111111111111111112)
Total Value: $0.00 (0 SOL)
Token Balances:
No tokens found with non-zero balance
```

**Expected behavior**

An RPC failure propagates as a typed error (`ElizaError` with a code such as `SOLANA_RPC_UNAVAILABLE`). `updateWalletData` distinguishes "RPC reported zero accounts" from "RPC did not answer" and never caches the latter. The provider renders an explicit unavailable state instead of a zero portfolio. `GET /wallet/balance` returns the route's existing 500 path instead of `balance: 0`. The 429 retry is bounded by an attempt counter and ends in a typed `RATE_LIMITED` error.

- [x] Domain artifacts: cache rows and HTTP responses are shown inline above.

28:Tdca,## Summary

Three more serializers mark a value `"[Circular]"` whenever the same object was visited **anywhere earlier in the walk**, not only when it is an ancestor of the current value. Each keeps one `WeakSet` for the whole traversal and never removes an entry, so a graph that references one object from two places (a DAG) is rendered with the second reference replaced by the string. Same class as #25125 (logger), #30994 (JS runtime bridge), #30999 (settings debug).

| Site | Shape | Consumers |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts` `stringifyForDiagnostics` | `JSON.stringify` replacer with a global `seen` | `trajectory-recorder.ts`, `trajectory-utils.ts`, `services/evaluator.ts`, experience evaluators, relationships provider. Recorded trajectories and evaluator diagnostics therefore show `"[Circular]"` for a repeated sub-object in a model response or tool result. |
| `packages/ui/src/utils/tts-debug.ts` `serializeTtsDebugDetail` | same replacer shape | TTS debug log lines |
| `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/redact-sensitive-data.ts` `redactValue` | recursive walker, `seen.add` without `delete` | currently exercised only by `truncate-suffix-reserve.test.ts` |

## Reproduction (develop `822aba345b67`)

```ts
const shared = { id: 1 };
stringifyForDiagnostics({ a: shared, b: shared });
// → {"a":{"id":1},"b":"[Circular]"}
```

The same input through the other two helpers yields the same shape.

## Expected

Only a reference back to an open ancestor is a cycle. For the two `JSON.stringify` replacers the ancestor path is recoverable from the replacer's `this` (the holder object): pop the stack until the top is the holder, then test membership. For the recursive walker, add before descending and delete in `finally`.


## Consolidated scope — maintainer backlog review, 2026-09-14

Also owns #30999 (settings-debug shared references) and #31113 (log redaction shared references). These are the same ancestor-path versus globally visited-set defect, with distinct output contracts. Candidate PRs are #31005, #31002 and #31119; they are not approved by this consolidation. A coordinated implementation should avoid duplicating incompatible traversal logic, while retaining sink-specific secret redaction, explicit diagnostic preview limits and genuine cycle handling.

Acceptance must include sibling aliases, repeated array entries, true cycles, nested Errors and secret-bearing values at the actual consumers. Check expansion cost on shared graphs. **Describe the bug**

The English UI renders the *names* of 37 translation keys instead of their text. The character editor's unsaved-changes dialog is titled "Unsaved Changes Title" with the body "Unsaved Changes Body"; its counts read "3 Conversation Count" and "2 Post Count"; the computer-use approval overlay's title is "Title", its body "Body", its mode line "Mode Line"; the browser workspace's blocked-frame card says "Frame Blocked Title" / "Frame Blocked Description"; a dialog button reads "Dont Save".

Two things combine on `origin/develop` (`1045297cca`):

1. `packages/ui/src/i18n/index.ts:48` resolves `localized[key] ?? english[key] ?? defaultValue ?? key`, so the catalog value in `locales/en.json` always wins over the `defaultValue` the call site passes. That is by design (the catalog is the source of truth), and `check-i18n.mjs` enforces that every literal key is catalogued.
2. When the catalog was backfilled, these 37 keys were catalogued with their own Title-Cased key name as the value — `"charactereditor.UnsavedChangesTitle": "Unsaved Changes Title"` — while every call site passes the real English as `defaultValue`. Because of (1), the real English never renders.

**To Reproduce**

Through the real translator, no mocks (`packages/ui`, `bun run` of a six-line script):

```

charactereditor.UnsavedChangesBody   "You have unsaved changes. Save before switching?"
                                                       ->  "Unsaved Changes Body"
computeruseapprovaloverlay.ModeLine  "Approval mode: {{mode}}." (mode: "auto")
                                                       ->  "Mode Line"
charactereditor.ConversationCount    "conversations"    ->  "Conversation Count"
charactereditor.DontSave             "Don't save"       ->  "Dont Save"
browserworkspace.FrameBlockedTitle   "Open this site in your browser"
                                                       ->  "Frame Blocked Title"
```

In the app: open the character editor, edit any field, switch character — the dialog is titled "Unsaved Changes Title". Trigger a computer-use approval — the overlay is titled "Title".

Scan of every literal `t("key", { defaultValue: "…" })` call site under `packages/ui/src` (4,300 sites) against `en.json`: 37 keys whose catalog value is the key's own name while the call site supplies different text — either a multi-word label degraded to Title Case ("Drag To Reorder", "Dont Save") or a structural name standing in for a sentence or a placeholder-bearing string ("Reset Confirm Body", "Agent Send Warning", "Mode Line").

- Tests cover stale configured override, valid configured override, provider warming, and default fallback.2f:T1428,## Summary
MeetingService.requestJoin claims to "Reserve the meeting SYNCHRONOUSLY before the first await" so two concurrent same-URL joins cannot both slip past the `already_joined` duplicate check (invariant MJ-4, single-bot-per-meeting). That guarantee only holds when billing is NOT configured. When `this.deps.createBillingSession` is wired (the metered/cloud deployments), `await billing.reserveInitial()` runs BETWEEN the duplicate check (`const duplicate = [...this.sessions.values()].find(...)`) and the actual reservation insert (`this.sessions.set(sessionId, session)`). Because that await yields the event loop before any session is recorded, two concurrent requestJoin calls for the same meeting both observe an empty session map, both reserve billing, and both launch a bot. Impact on the exact deployments that have billing: duplicate bot attendance in one meeting, two separate transcript rows created for the same call, and double credit reservation/consumption — precisely the failure the reservation comment says is prevented.

## Reproduction
```
Added a Vitest mirroring the existing MJ-4 test but with billing wired (two FakeMeetingBillingSession queued via scriptedDeps), then fired two concurrent requestJoin calls for the same meeting URL:

  const { deps } = scriptedDeps([new ScriptedAdapter("google_meet")], [new FakeMeetingBillingSession(), new FakeMeetingBillingSession()]);
  const service = new MeetingService(fake.runtime, deps);
  const results = await Promise.allSettled([
    service.requestJoin({ platform: "google_meet", meetingUrl: MEET_URL }),
    service.requestJoin({ platform: "google_meet", meetingUrl: "https://meet.google.com/abcdefghij" }),
  ]);

Command: `npx vitest run src/toctou-billing.repro.test.ts` (from plugins/plugin-meetings).
Observed: `fulfilled: 2 live sessions: 2`; two "[MeetingService] meeting join requested" logs with two different sessionIds for the same nativeMeetingId `abc-defg-hij`; two transcript records created. Test assertion `expect(fulfilled).toHaveLength(1)` fails (got 2).
Expected (matches the no-billing MJ-4 test at service.test.ts:139): exactly one fulfilled, one rejected with code `already_joined`, one live session, one bot launched.
The scratch test was removed after confirming the failure.
```

## Proposed fix
Make the reservation truly synchronous with the duplicate check by inserting the session into `this.sessions` BEFORE the first await (before `await billing.reserveInitial()`), instead of at the current `this.sessions.set(sessionId, session)` that follows the billing await. Concretely: build the session record (or a minimal non-terminal reservation placeholder keyed by sessionId with the platform/nativeMeetingId and status "requested") and `this.sessions.set` it immediately after the dup-check in the same synchronous turn; then run `await billing.reserveInitial()` and the rest of setup against the already-claimed slot. Extend the existing rollback: if billing.reserveInitial (or later world/room/writer setup) throws, `this.sessions.delete(sessionId)` so the meeting stays joinable (mirrors the current catch that releases the reservation). Test plan (>=10 lines): add a billing-enabled variant of the MJ-4 concurrency test asserting exactly one fulfilled join, one `already_joined` rejection, one live session, one adapter launch, and exactly one billing reserveInitial consumed; plus a test that a failing reserveInitial rolls the reservation back so a subsequent join of the same meeting succeeds (not permanently `already_joined`).

## Evidence plan
PR attaches the failing-before/passing-after billing concurrency test output (fulfilled count 2→1, live sessions 2→1) and the rollback test, run via `npx vitest run` in plugins/plugin-meetings, alongside the unchanged existing no-billing MJ-4 test to show no regression.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@d8c5b3124d97fac8189ad49afc14363ba56764f6:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->30:T1083,## Summary
In the EVM swap action, `buildSwapDetails` turns a relative-amount intent ("half", "max", "percent") into a concrete amount using the wallet's NATIVE chain balance, regardless of which token is being sold. The swap template explicitly instructs the model to classify phrasings like "swap 30% of my USDC" / "half"/"all" as relative modes for the input token, but the code computes that fraction of the native gas token balance instead of the ERC-20 fromToken balance. Result: a user asking to "swap half my USDC" trades an amount equal to half of their ETH balance denominated in USDC — an arbitrary, wrong quantity (far too small if ETH balance is small, or a different value entirely). This is a silent financial correctness defect: the transaction succeeds but moves the wrong amount. `WalletProvider.getWalletBalances()` only returns per-chain native balances (`formatUnits(balance, 18)` from `getBalance`), so no token balance is ever consulted for the relative computation.

## Reproduction
```
Added a focused test at plugins/plugin-wallet/src/chains/evm/actions/swap.reltoken-repro.test.ts driving the real `buildSwapDetails` with a stub WalletProvider whose native `base` balance is "2.0" and a stub runtime whose model returns {inputToken:"USDC", outputToken:"WETH", amountMode:"percent", amountPercent:50, chain:"base"}.
Command: node_modules/.bin/vitest run plugins/plugin-wallet/src/chains/evm/actions/swap.reltoken-repro.test.ts
Observed: PASS — details.fromToken === "USDC" and details.amount === "1". "1" is 50% of the 2.0 NATIVE balance, proving the 50%-of-USDC request was resolved against the ETH balance rather than the USDC balance. Expected: the amount should be 50% of the caller's USDC balance. (Test file removed after running; it is not part of the proposed diff’s permanent surface beyond the new regression test.)
```

## Proposed fix
In buildSwapDetails (plugins/plugin-wallet/src/chains/evm/actions/swap.ts): when amountMode is relative and the resolved fromToken is not the chain's native currency, resolve the fraction against the fromToken's on-chain balance (ERC-20 balanceOf → formatUnits with the token's decimals) instead of the native `chainBalance`. Keep the native-balance basis (with the existing 0.9 gas reserve for "max") only when fromToken is the native asset. If the token balance cannot be fetched, throw EVMError(INVALID_PARAMS) rather than silently using the native balance. Test plan (>=10 lines): unit-test buildSwapDetails for (a) percent swap of an ERC-20 → amount equals the fraction of the token balance, (b) native "max" still applies the 0.9 reserve, (c) native "half" halves the native balance, (d) token balance unavailable → throws. Stub WalletProvider + runtime model output as in the repro.

## Evidence plan
PR attaches the failing-before/passing-after regression test (real buildSwapDetails, stubbed provider/runtime, no network) plus a short trace showing amount for a percent USDC swap changing from the native-derived value to the token-derived value.

## Duplicate search
gh search issues --repo elizaOS/eliza --state all for: "wallet swap relative amount balance", "swap percent native balance", "resolveRelativeAmount", "swap half token balance" — all empty. gh search prs "swap relative amount" — empty. Cross-checked our swarm: gh pr list --author agent-cortex --state open and gh issue list --author agent-cortex --state open — the only wallet items are #29565 (SpendingPolicy RollingSpendCap) and #29562/#29564 (plugin-video captions); none touch relative swap amount resolution. No duplicate.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@2a4af7351c96881b83333fabb37927222dbb09fd:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->31:Tcf1,## Defect

The observed staging incident is a stale-enabled orphan reconciler, not the provision catch path. Four healthy generations with the same deterministic name were removed by direct Docker force-removal-shaped events after the reconciler five-minute grace window, despite terminal lifecycle jobs and fresh heartbeats. The deploy preserves `/opt/eliza/cloud/.env.local`, so an old `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=1` survives even though the daemon code defaults the feature off.

A separate code-proven latent race exists in post-create failure cleanup: the provision catch retains an exact Docker handle but discards its immutable container ID and replacement attempt when it falls back to `stopForReplacement(handle.sandboxId)`. Because the sandbox ID is the reusable container name, delayed cleanup can resolve a healthy successor.

## Production-like evidence

One staging sandbox produced four healthy same-name containers with distinct immutable Docker IDs and `ai.elizaos.replacement-attempt` labels. Docker delivered direct signal 9 events with no preceding stop/SIGTERM after each container had exceeded the five-minute grace window.

The fourth generation started at 23:57:14Z, wrote a fresh serving-row update at 00:03:51Z, then received signal 9 at 00:04:07Z and was destroyed at 00:04:08Z, about 412 seconds after start. Its database row was still `running`, carried the expected node and VPN address, and no destructive lifecycle job was active. A node-health sweep had updated at 00:03:13Z; the kill followed about 54 seconds later. The preceding generation had the same `sweep + 54 seconds -> SIGKILL` shape. This is the orphan phase signature, although the underlying read-authority mismatch that classified a live row as orphaned still requires separate investigation.

## Scope

- Make the provisioning-worker deploy overwrite stale host state with repository-owned `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0`, verify it before restart, and verify it again in the health gate.
- Route failed Docker provision cleanup through its exact node, immutable container ID, replacement attempt, and VPN identity.
- Validate the expected agent/container identity and fail closed when exact Docker cleanup identity is incomplete.
- Preserve mutable sandbox-ID cleanup only for non-Docker providers.
- Do not add or mutate database schema.

## Acceptance

- A deploy rewrites a seeded host `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=1` to exactly one `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0` entry and refuses health if it drifts.
- A stale cleanup for an old Docker ID cannot stop or remove a healthy successor that reuses the same container name.
- Cleanup still stops/removes the exact target when its immutable ID and attempt label match.
- Retry/absence stays idempotent and never targets the current name occupant.
- Focused cloud-shared and deployment reconciliation tests, typecheck, and Biome pass.

## Evidence plan

- Execute the deployment `EnvironmentFile` block verbatim against a stale seeded host file.
- Drive the real Docker identity resolver and scripted SSH transport across same-name reuse.
- Prove at service level that the provision catch passes exact handle identity and never invokes mutable-name Docker teardown.
- N/A UI, frontend, LLM, audio, and visual evidence: backend lifecycle and deployment configuration only.
32:T948,## Concrete defect

The hosted MCP tool index caps BM25 results before filtering by platform. `Tier2ToolIndex.search` retrieves `(offset + limit) * 2` results across all platforms, filters that prefix, then applies the requested page. The requested platform can therefore return an empty page despite having matching tools in the index. Increasing the fixed multiplier cannot guarantee correct filtered pagination.

The actual consumer is `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/eliza/plugin-mcp/actions/mcp.ts:302`: the `search_actions` operation forwards the requested query, platform, limit and offset to this index.

## Verified reproduction

35:T156f,## Summary
SwabbleWeb.start() (Web Speech fallback in plugins/plugin-native-swabble/src/web.ts) assigns this.recognition, then `await`s startAudioLevelMonitoring() (which itself awaits getUserMedia) BEFORE calling recognition.start(). getUserMedia is always asynchronous, so any stop() that runs during that await interleaves: stop() sets isActive=false, calls this.recognition.stop() (a no-op, recognition not started yet), sets this.recognition=null, and calls stopAudioLevelMonitoring() while the level-meter stream/interval do not yet exist. When start() then resumes it creates this.mediaStream + a 100ms setInterval and calls recognition.start(), whose onstart sets isActive=true and re-emits state "listening". The net result after a start()+stop() sequence: the microphone MediaStream stays live (browser mic indicator on — privacy leak), the audioLevel interval fires forever, and the SpeechRecognition instance keeps running and auto-restarting via onend (isActive===true) — yet this.recognition is null, so no later stop() can ever free it. isListening() reports {listening:true} after the consumer already called stop(). This is a state-machine/teardown-survival race distinct from the fatal-recognition-error leak fixed by merged PR #22656 (that fix only tears down on onerror/onend, neither of which fires here).

## Reproduction
```
In plugins/plugin-native-swabble (node_modules present) I added a probe vitest reusing the file's FakeRecognition harness plus minimal AudioContext/analyser mocks and a getUserMedia returning a deferred promise wrapping a stream whose track.stop() is spied. Sequence: const p = plugin.start({config:{triggers:["eliza"]}}); await Promise.resolve(); await plugin.stop(); resolveGum(stream); await p. Then assert plugin.isListening() === {listening:false}, FakeRecognition.latest.running === false, and track.stopped === true. Observed via `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run src/probe-race.test.ts`: `AssertionError: expected { listening: true } to deeply equal { listening: false }` — the plugin remains listening with recognition.running true and the mic track never stopped, after the consumer already called stop(). Expected: a start()+stop() sequence must leave the plugin fully torn down (not listening, recognition stopped, mic released). Probe file removed after the run; `git status --short plugins/` is clean.
```

## Proposed fix
In start()'s Web Speech branch, guard against a stop() that raced the getUserMedia await. Capture the intended recognition instance before the await and, after `await this.startAudioLevelMonitoring()`, detect supersession: `if (this.recognition !== recognition) { this.stopAudioLevelMonitoring(); return { started: false }; }` before calling recognition.start(). This releases the level-meter stream/interval that startAudioLevelMonitoring created during the await and avoids starting an unreferenceable recognizer. (Equivalently, a monotonically increasing startToken/`stopping` flag set by stop() and checked after the await.) Scope is ~4 lines in one file, no contract change. Test plan (>=10 lines, extends existing web.test.ts harness): (1) race teardown — deferred getUserMedia, call start() then stop() before resolving, resolve the stream, assert isListening() false, the FakeRecognition never left running, and the mic track.stop spy was called; (2) normal path preserved — start() fully awaited then a valid final speech result still emits transcript/wakeWord and isListening() true; (3) idempotency — a second stop() after the race does not throw. All assertions run against the real SwabbleWeb, no stubbing of the unit under test.

## Evidence plan
The PR will attach failing-before/passing-after vitest output for the new race test in plugins/plugin-native-swabble/src/web.test.ts (before: isListening()==={listening:true}, recognition running, mic track.stop never called; after: {listening:false}, recognition not running, mic track stopped), plus `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-native-swabble test` (all existing tests still green), typecheck, and lint. It will show the regression test for the normal awaited start→speech→stop path remains green to prove no behavior change for correct callers.

## Duplicate search
gh search issues --repo elizaOS/eliza: "swabble stop race" (0), "SwabbleWeb start stop" (0), "swabble startAudioLevelMonitoring" (0), "swabble recognition running after stop" (0), "swabble microphone leak" → only #22655 (CLOSED). Reviewed #22655/#22656: that report+merged fix addresses the microphone/level-meter leak on a *fatal recognition error* (onerror not in {no-speech,aborted} then onend); its fix hardens onerror/onend teardown. My defect never triggers onerror/onend — recognition.start() succeeds and keeps running — so it is not covered by #22656. No issue/PR describes the start()/stop() interleave race.

_Filed under a standing operator authorization for the autonomous audit pipeline, after local reproduction and duplicate search._

38:T2552,## Summary

This PR implements the code findings from the compliance research review (`compliance-research-2026-09-18`) across core, agent, cloud and app/ui, re-checked against HEAD. It also removes about 2,500 lines of dead or duplicate code.

### core
- A typed modality table drives PII swap and binary/text handling. `TEXT_EMBEDDING_BATCH`, `TEXT_TO_SPEECH` and `PII_SCRUB` are no longer pseudonymized.
- A host-only processing policy extends `ConfidentialInferenceAuthority`. It admits each attempt before the secret/PII swap, the hooks and the handler.
  - A denial is terminal: it never fails over, the embedding queue doesn't retry it, and it isn't retryable when a model call inside an action handler raises it.
  - The policy sees the model type the caller asked for, before any LLM-mode override.
  - With no policy installed, behavior is unchanged.
- `runActionsByMode` and plugin-assistant plan steps now settle through the action gate (`settleActionHandler`).
- Removed, with zero in-repo callers: the `zod-schema.agent-runtime` subpath, `ToolPolicyService` / `getFilteredActions` / `isActionAllowed`, 17 private model wrappers in `runtime.ts`, and duplicate helpers. `canonicalJson` and `parseBooleanFromText` now each have one implementation. The shared canonical backup JSON rejects sparse array holes.

### agent
- `ELIZA_PROTECTED_PROFILE=dstack-cpu` (captured from the process env at entry) runs admission before config load, vault hydration, API listen and plugin resolution.
- `ELIZA_TEE_*`, `ELIZA_DSTACK_*`, `ELIZA_CONFIDENTIAL_*` and `ELIZA_PROTECTED_*` are env-only. They are blocked from config and API env writes, and the state-dir `config.env` can't apply or persist them either.
- Evidence-factory registration is refused under the profile.
- Audit sinks are async, and a failure in a required sink now rejects. Retention never deletes audit log types. Confidential-runtime schema is v2.

### cloud
- API key create, delete, regenerate and mobile revoke write their audit event in the same transaction. A sink failure rolls the change back.
  - An oversized client `X-Request-Id` is dropped from the event instead of failing the write.
- PII scrub markers record their inspection scope (migration `0506`).
- There is a single retryable provider-fallback middleware with a `ProviderFallbackPolicy` hook. A refusal throws `PROVIDER_FALLBACK_REFUSED`.
- With `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_REQUIRED`, agent env secrets no longer fall back to plaintext. `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_REQUIRE_AAD` is documented as off until writers pass coordinates.
- New `user_consents` table (migration `0507`) with `GET`/`POST /api/v1/me/consents` for `vision_capture`. GET also returns `effective` (the enforced policy, with `basis` and `defaultGranted`) and `capture.modelCallRecording` (the deployment's recording policy).
  - There's a user-scoped live `POST /api/v1/me/data-export` that requires a recent login.
- A single `getRequestIp` helper (`cf-connecting-ip` first) serves all webhooks. Expired `auth_events` are purged.

### app / ui
- There is one session-cookie parser: identical duplicate cookies are accepted, conflicting ones rejected.
- Logout revokes every presented session credential, including conflicting duplicates, and also expires the `Domain` variant.
- Revoking a session closes its open WebSockets (1008). Admission re-reads the session after binding, so a revoke that races the handshake can't leave a socket open.
- The privacy panel shows the deployment's model-call recording policy (read-only) and leaves vision/screen capture to device permissions. It uses the live export endpoint.
- Removed the dead first-run fork, update policy, `api/response` and `globals` shims.

### Review feedback addressed
- Migration collisions: renumbered to `0506_pii_scrub_markers_inspection_scope`, `0507_user_consents` and `0508_app_notification_secret_envelope_v2`. These are the filenames present in the current branch.
- The sparse-array bug in canonical backup JSON is fixed.
- The consents API exposes the effective policy and default, as distinct from an explicit grant.

### Model-call recording, session controls and attestation
**Recording (no consent; off in production):**
- The cloud records billed model calls under one policy: `LLM_TRAJECTORY_CAPTURE=on|off`, otherwise off in production and on elsewhere. The `trajectory_training` consent purpose is removed.
- Payloads never go to the public `BLOB` bucket. They use a dedicated private store when one is bound, otherwise Postgres.
- Bodies are encrypted with row-bound org field encryption.
- `LLM_TRAJECTORY_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 90) sets retention, enforced by a daily purge.
- Account deletion removes payload objects by org prefix, orphans included.
- The agent's trajectory gate defaults off under `ELIZA_PROTECTED_PROFILE`. Operators can still enable it inside the TEE.

**Session and data safeguards:**
- **Automatic logoff:** the protected profile gets a 30-minute idle logoff and a 12h absolute cap. `ELIZA_SESSION_IDLE_MINUTES` sets the idle window in any profile. The idle window slides only on real user activity (`x-eliza-last-activity` from the shared UI clients, allowed in CORS for remote agents), never on background polling or sockets.
- **Password change:** revokes every other session and closes its sockets.
- **Auth store:** row counts no longer assume success.
- **Media:** under the protected profile, media is served only after authentication. Same-origin cookie auth keeps `<img>`/`<audio>` working.
- **Field encryption:**
  - Row-bound writes use an `enc:v2` envelope that authenticates its AAD flag.
  - Unbound writes keep the legacy v1 bytes for old readers.
  - `SECRETS_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS` plus a guarded DEK rewrap enable master-key rotation.
  - Migration `0508` lets the billing notification guard accept v2.
- **Audit logs:** the audit-log purge cron now actually runs (7-year retention).

**Attestation implementation and emulator validation (hardware acceptance pending):**
- **In-repo verification:** dstack v1 attestations and TDX v4/v5 quotes are parsed in-repo. The raw report_data binding, the TD debug bit, the quote's self-signature and register agreement are checked independently of the pinned external verifier.
- **Hardware-free harness:** a fake guest-agent socket, a pinned stub verifier and an NRAS emulator drive the real admission code to success and through a rejection matrix. The matrix includes dstack simulator output.
- **GPU attestation:** NVIDIA confidential-GPU attestation goes through NRAS, and the ES384 EAT tokens are verified locally against NVIDIA's CA. GPU claims can only come from verified evidence bound to the TDX nonce.
- **Key release:** the agent appraises its own evidence before trusting any KMS. A dstack `GetKey` client verifies the KMS signature chain to a pinned root.
- **Satisfiable topologies:** the state-volume, `tdx-cpu` and GPU weight topologies and the dstack cloud inference profile require only claims that dstack and NRAS evidence actually establish.
- **Test lanes:** `test:tee:dstack` (hardware-free) and `test:tee:dstack-live`, which is opt-in for a real CVM, the dstack simulator and H100 CC GPUs.

The model-weight unseal helpers are available, but the production model loader does not yet call `prepareConfidentialModelWeights`. This PR does not establish encrypted-weight loading in the shipped inference path or regulatory compliance.

**Deploy order:**
- Apply migration `0508` before deploying the Worker. Billing notification secrets are written as `enc:v2`.
- Roll out readers before relying on `SECRETS_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS`.

### Follow-ups
- **Processing policy:** before any host installs a policy, first-party actions need an `egress` declaration, including `egress: []` for the security evaluator hook. Otherwise every action is denied as `destination_undeclared`. No host installs a policy today.
- **PII swap:** it now skips `TEXT_TO_SPEECH` and `PII_SCRUB`, so a remote TTS provider receives real names. Confirm this is intended, or scope the skip to local providers.
- **`FIELD_ENCRYPTION_REQUIRE_AAD`:** it stays off until agent env secrets can be row-bound. They are encrypted before their row id exists.
- **Real hardware:** running `test:tee:dstack-live` on a dstack TDX CVM (and an H100 CC GPU) needs infrastructure.

## Verification (Node 24.15.0, Bun 1.4.2, merged with develop `4778fe302d5`)
- Root `bun run verify` passes on the merged tree (284/284 turbo tasks, all audits).
- core: full suite earlier in this PR; `trajectory-gate` passes (5).
- cloud-shared: 56 targeted tests pass: capture policy, trajectory storage and encryption, field encryption, account-deletion payloads and adapters, fallback policy, PII scrub. The 15 app-billing/generic-billing Postgres suites pass (45 pass; the rest skip without a live Postgres).
- cloud-api: consents/export, API-key audit transaction and PII scrub route pass (13).
- ui: account-security, auth/activity and client tests pass (77 before the final merge; account-security 52 after it).
- app: auth store, session idle/protected-profile/cookie, browser-session WebSocket and password-change revocation pass (52).
- agent (real end-to-end): TDX/dstack admission, protected profile, GPU attestation, live lane (skipped without hardware), protected media, media capability, TEE env boundary and audit durability pass (10 files). `scripts/tee-full-stack-local.ts` passes.
- `check-migration-prefix-order` passes. The router was regenerated.

3a:T3018,Cloud billing: dispute credit units, bigint defaults, phone-webhook route test, container catalogue pin, mini-app removal, and an end-to-end Plus/Pro subscription audit. One commit per logical change.

## Fixes #31449: dispute reinstatement restores credit units
`handleChargeDisputeFundsReinstated` computed `min(dispute.amount/100, |clawback|)`, which compares provider dollars against credit units. A $10 / 500-credit pack got 10 credits back. It now restores the matched clawback's applied credit amount. That amount is already scaled to the disputed fraction and capped at the balance, so an unrecovered shortfall is never restored. The replay key, org ownership and out-of-order retry are unchanged.
- New `api/__tests__/stripe-dispute-reinstatement.pglite.test.ts` drives the real `processStripeEvent` and credits service on PGlite. It covers a full pack, partial consumption, a partial $3 dispute, a legacy 1:1 top-up, an empty balance, replays, early-reinstatement retry and bystander isolation. 5/5 pass; it fails on develop.

## Fixes #31523: bigint defaults break `db:generate`
Seven `.default(0n)` / `5n*1024n**3` defaults are now `sql\`0\`` / `sql\`5368709120\``, so database defaults are unchanged. New `shared/src/db/schema-snapshot-serialization.test.ts` builds the drizzle-kit snapshot and DDL over the whole schema and pins the seven defaults. It passes, and fails with develop's BigInt default.

## Refs #31768: Twilio and Blooio route-level idempotency test
The route fix itself already landed on develop in #31775 (`processOnce`), so this PR only adds coverage. `api/__tests__/phone-webhook-idempotency.pglite.test.ts` uses a real PGlite idempotency store. Three concurrent deliveries of one message route once, and a failed delivery releases its claim so the retry routes again, for both providers. 4/4 pass on develop's routes; against the pre-#31775 routes it routed 3 times.

## Refs #22957: container catalogue
The ghost container constants and the shutdown-email monthly figure were already removed by #32799. This ports the rest of #26383's intent: `container-warning-rate.test.ts` now pins the catalogue key set to `DAILY_RUNNING_COST` and `SHUTDOWN_WARNING_HOURS`, so an unmetered rate can't come back. The tracker's consolidated decisions remain open and need owner-picked values:
- storage units, price and fallback (#22956)
- API-key price, count and rate (#22958)
- MCP buyer/creator settlement (#22961)
- legacy packs and minimum top-up (#22963)
- which credit sources count toward RPM tiers (#23019)
- affiliate surcharge disclosure (#23020)
- frozen/sleeping retention (#22967)
- crypto/x402 refunds (#22968)
- media rejected by storage quota (#20956)

## Refs #32021: mini-app charge surface removed
Removed:
- `/apps/:id/charges/**` and `/app-credits/checkout|verify` routes
- `app-charge-{requests,settlement,callbacks}` services
- the public app-charge page and its i18n keys
- SDK methods and types
- the orchestrator `apps.charges.*` broker commands
- mock routes, scenarios and e2e charge tests

The crypto cron no longer drains app-charge callbacks. No tables or migrations are dropped.

**Money safety:** Stripe `checkout.session.completed` and `payment_intent.succeeded` that carry legacy mini-app metadata (`charge_request_id`, `miniapp_app`, `app_credit_purchase`) are acked with `retired_miniapp_payment` and never fulfilled as a generic credit purchase. OxaPay confirmation throws a typed `RETIRED_MINIAPP_PAYMENT` before any write. Tests: `stripe-retired-miniapp.pglite` (4/4, including a crediting control case and replays) and `crypto-retired-miniapp.pglite` (3/3).

**Kept, because non-mini-app features use it:**
- App registration and auth.
- App inference credit reservations used by chat completions, messages and admission recovery.
- Earnings and monetization settings.
- `/app-credits/balance`.
- Payment requests and x402.
- App-billing (per-app subscriptions and trials). Org subscription tables have foreign keys to `app_billing_scopes`, account deletion runs app-billing dispositions, plain app sign-up materializes app-billing accounts, and chat/embeddings inference calls `admitAppSubscriptionInference`.

**Behaviour change to review:** sensitive-request payment links now point at the live payment-request page (`/payment/<id>` on the site URL) instead of the deleted app-charge page.

## Plus/Pro subscription audit (webhooks)
- **Terminal publish (lockout):** a subscription Stripe cancels after failed payments could never be published, because Stripe advances the period on a failed renewal. The DB stayed `active` and checkout refused to resubscribe. The terminal publish now accepts Stripe's advanced period; identity fields must still match.
- **Dunning owner:** `invoice.payment_failed` and live past_due/unpaid now publish dunning with a 3-day grace. A paid renewal clears it, and recovery handles it too.
- **Entitlement lapse:** the quota policy applies a 3-day grace after period end at read time. A lapsed or past-grace org gets the free tier instead of `ORGANIZATION_POLICY_UNAVAILABLE`.
- **Routing:** events route on the live subscription status, not the payload.
- **Events no longer thrown away:**
  - unowned subscription events are acked as `unhandled_subscription_event`
  - renewal/dashboard `customer.subscription.updated` is acked as `no_owned_change`
  - permanent typed failures are acked, with code and reason logged
- **Incidents instead of silent lapse:** out-of-band plan change, external cancel, trial/pause/pending updates, refunds/disputes on subscription invoices, and strict-validation renewal rejections.
- **Basil payloads:** `invoice.paid` reads the subscription from the fetched invoice. An unexpected `api_version` is logged.
- **Queue:** retries get exponential backoff and a drain no longer re-reads a message it just requeued. On dead-letter, the ingress `webhook_events` row is deleted so a Stripe resend can re-enter.
- **Recovery:** already-published terminal subscriptions are skipped, and per-subscription degradation no longer 503s the whole cron.

## Plus/Pro subscription audit (checkout, portal, status, UI, SDK)
- **Customer Portal:** new `POST /api/v1/subscriptions/portal`.
  - Gated to billing managers; the customer comes from the DB; the return URL is built on the server.
  - Locked configuration: no plan switching, cancel at period end only.
  - past_due and unpaid orgs can use it.
- **Replays:** an APPLIED replay returns `completed` only while that subscription is live (otherwise `stale_intent`). FAILED/SUPERSEDED replays return `expired`.
- **Different plan while a checkout is pending:** the open session is expired and the new checkout continues, unless the old session already completed.
- **Stuck commands:** PREPARED or started-without-session commands are superseded or failed instead of blocking the org.
- **Sweeper:** a stale-checkout sweeper runs in the Stripe queue cron. Account deletion closes open sessions.
- **Stripe session:** 23h55m expiry, `currency: "usd"`, and prices with `currency_options` are rejected.
- **Org scope:** the org checkout and command queries, and `enqueueCommand`'s conflict check, ignore app-scoped commands.
- **Plans route:** rate limited, with a 5 s cache of typed failures.
- **Error mapping:** non-retryable checkout reasons map to 409/403.
- **Snapshot:** now has `subscriptionId` and a cancel/undo control.
- **UI:**
  - A status card shows plan, status, renew/cancel date, grace and allowance.
  - It has cancel/undo with polling and the portal button.
  - Subscribe is hidden for non-managers and live subscribers.
  - The checkout key is per user, and localStorage access is guarded.
  - The session id is dropped from the URL after confirm, and a cancel-return banner is shown.
- **SDK:** new `startSubscriptionCheckout`, `confirmSubscriptionCheckout` and `createSubscriptionPortalSession`. The unused `SubscriptionDto` / `SubscriptionPublicState` are removed, which is a breaking export change.

## Policy questions for the owner
1. Plus/Pro trials: not implemented (owner decision). Enabling them would need a per-plan eligibility table and a relaxed `trialing` constraint.
2. Is 3 days right for both the renewal and dunning grace, and should past_due within grace keep paid access?
3. Should a refund or dispute on a subscription invoice revoke that period's entitlement or allowance? Currently it only opens an incident.
4. Should an out-of-band price swap or a dashboard/portal cancel be adopted into our records, or stay incident-only?
5. Plan switching (Plus to Pro): currently unsupported, and Subscribe is hidden while a live subscription exists.
6. The cancellation contract says "billing owner, recent auth", but the code allows owner or admin without recent auth.
7. Cancel/undo is rejected during dunning.
8. Paid checkout on an org fenced for deletion: refund, or manual review? Currently logged `subscription_paid_checkout_unentitled` and kept pending.
9. The retry budget (8 attempts, about 2 h) and a DLQ replay tool.
10. Recovery no longer scans canceled subscriptions. A corrupt projection on one is caught only by a late event.
11. Renewal-validation rejections (discount/tax/balance/proration): keep acking with an incident, or keep the org funded while ops reviews?
12. Latent: the live-subscription guard ignores `trialing`/`paused`. This only matters if trials are added.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
3b:T1ccc,Fixes the Cloud Plus/Pro subscription defects found by the subscription audit, covering allowance-first funding (follow-up to #23092, closed) and entitlement-derived quotas (follow-up to #23094, closed). Webhook/checkout/portal/billing-UI work and the past_due/grace entitlement projection (A6, B2) are handled separately on `shaw/fix-cloud-billing-subscriptions` and are not touched here.

## B1: paid plans could not create any resource (critical)
Plus/Pro entitlements set every resource ceiling to `null`, and the quota policy treats `null` as unavailable, so subscribers were denied new apps, characters, containers, sandboxes and storage writes.
- The v1 catalog now declares explicit `resourceCeilings`, validated so a paid plan is never below the Free ceilings. Until larger paid ceilings are ratified, paid ceilings equal Free (5 characters, 5 sandboxes, 1 container, 5 GiB, 25 apps).
- Entitlement derivation reads the ceilings from the catalog. Migration `0474` republishes them onto existing paid projections so stored rows keep matching their derivation (the quota policy compares the two).
- The public plan DTO (shared + SDK) exposes the ceilings instead of `null`.

## A1: allowance-first funding for every allowance-eligible spend path
Each path now funds subscribers allowance first, then purchased credit, through `subscriptionFundingService`; everyone else keeps the unchanged cash lane.
- New router `lib/services/allowance-first-credits.ts` for request-scoped callers: search proxy, DexScreener proxy (now settles or releases its prepaid hold instead of refunding cash), meeting transcription, pending video holds and A2A chat/image/memory/conversation.
- Pending video holds persist their funding identity; the video reconcile sweep settles funded holds through subscription funding.
- Native storage PUTs and paid reads (migration `0475` adds a tenant-bound `funding_reservation_id` and rewrites the shape checks/read triggers).
- Container daily/terminal billing (earnings first, then allowance, then credits) and container deploy debits (migration `0476`).
- Legacy hourly agent billing and the agent operation biller (migration `0477`).
- Money stays exact (canonical 6-decimal strings / bigint micros) and every funding key is retry-stable, so replays are idempotent.

## A2: debit-boundary ratchet in CI
`subscription-funding-debit-boundaries.test.ts` fails when production debit signals differ from the reviewed inventory, when a boundary's class disagrees with the policy, or when an allowance-eligible boundary does not route subscribers through allowance-first funding.

## A3: stranded reservations
Reservations persist the caller's own deadline (TTL or window end) instead of the allowance period expiry. A sweep in the existing `sweep-credit-reservations` cron releases stale holds to their exact sources with keyed refunds. Only owners without their own reconciler are swept; Dedicated windows, native storage and pending video jobs are excluded.

## A7: Dedicated stopped at every period boundary
A time-metered window that straddles the period end funds only its in-period share from allowance and the remainder from purchased credit; the window is no longer clipped to the period.

## A8: ex-subscribers on cash
An unfunded (canceled) subscription is cash-only for allowance-eligible work instead of a hard denial.

## A9: expire job
`expireEndedPeriods` retires ended periods (and periods of terminal subscriptions) with an append-only `expire` entry, from the same cron. Later settlements forfeit their remainder as `expired_refund`.

## A10: resubscribe after a terminal lifecycle event
Granting a new period retires an overlapping bucket left open by a terminal (canceled/incomplete_expired) predecessor instead of conflicting, and the expire job retires such buckets on the next tick. I did not add a call inside the webhook lifecycle finalizer (`subscription-billing-operations.ts`) to stay out of the webhook work in flight; it can call `subscriptionAllowanceRepository.retirePeriodInTransaction(tx, { reason: "subscription_terminal" })` after the entitlement rebuild if immediate retirement is wanted.

## B4, B5, B6: caches and warnings
- B4: a stale admission snapshot still fails its request closed, but the admission path now drops the isolate copy and republishes the shared projection at once, instead of ~30s of errors after every policy-generation bump.
- B5: the org tier cache compares its stamp with the durable policy generation, so subscription changes invalidate it without a writer hook.
- B6: the Dedicated low-funds warning counts remaining allowance.

## A13: minor issues
- Purchased-credit amounts that the numeric credit writer cannot represent exactly are rejected.
- Request ids outside the durable key grammar are hashed into `inference-gate:sha256.<hex>` instead of failing only subscription-funded requests.

## Policy questions (conservative behavior chosen)
- **A4, concurrent split:** kept the reserve-time split. Unused purchased credit is refunded at settle, but usage is not moved onto allowance that another request released later. Money never moves between sources after reservation.
- **A5, overage:** usage above a subscriber's reservation is still recorded as `uncollected_overage` and not charged. We never charge more than the held amount.
- **A11, mid-period plan change:** no proration. Allowance funding fails closed until the plan change is reconciled.
- **A12, trials:** platform trials still get no allowance; app-scope trials are unchanged.
- **B3, over-quota after downgrade:** existing resources are kept and only new create/start is blocked, per #23094.
- **Also decided:** stranded reservations are released (refunded) rather than charged, since there is no usage evidence. Paid ceilings equal Free until larger ceilings are ratified.
