3c:T31a6,Cloud agent lifecycle: safe restore gating, exact failed-provision cleanup, unpaid stop-in-place, automatic billing resume, lost-chargeback hold, and the Dedicated-to-Shared fallback authority.

One commit per issue. Stripe credit math and pricing files are untouched, which keeps this out of `shaw/fix-cloud-billing-subscriptions`. **Migration numbers:** this PR adds `0478`, `0479` and `0480`, after `0474` on develop. That leaves `0475`–`0477` for #32838; whichever lands second must re-sequence the journal `idx`/`when`.

## #29678: stale provision cleanup can kill a same-name successor

Fixes #29678

- **Deploy:** the provisioning-worker deploy now rewrites the preserved `/opt/eliza/cloud/.env.local` to exactly one repository-owned `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0`. It verifies the value before restart and again in the health gate.
- **Cleanup:** failed post-adoption provision cleanup no longer calls `stopForReplacement(handle.sandboxId)` by the reusable container name.
  - It re-fences the adopted candidate with its exact node, immutable container ID, replacement attempt and VPN identity (`fenceAdoptedProvisionForCleanup`).
  - It then retires the candidate through the existing durable replacement-cleanup authority, which releases the node slot exactly once.
  - Incomplete Docker identity fails closed as a retryable cleanup failure.
  - Name-based teardown remains only for non-Docker providers.
- **Remaining:** the orphan classifier's read-authority mismatch, where it treated a live `running` row as orphaned, still needs its own staging investigation with the reconciler disabled.

## #30697: preserve agent state and gate readiness until backup recovery succeeds

Fixes #30697

- **Fail on any known-backup failure:** every lookup, reconstruction or application failure for a known backup now fails the provision. Nothing prunes the chain; `degradeUnrecoverableSnapshot` and `pruneBackups(agentId, 0)` are gone.
  - Transient failures keep their cause so the job retries.
  - Failures that cannot heal become `SNAPSHOT_RESTORE_REQUIRES_FRESH_BOOT_CONSENT`. These are crypto errors, 401/403, 404/410, a custom image without the restore endpoint, null reconstruction, and size refusal.
- **Readiness gate:** every provision stays `provisioning` (non-routable) until the restore tail commits readiness through a generation-fenced CAS. Billing re-entry happens only after the restore is applied.
- **Background reconciler:** the stuck-provisioning reconciler no longer flips a healthy row to `running` while the agent has a backup that was never proven applied.
- **Unchanged paths:** first creation without a backup and explicit `forceFreshBoot` consent still work and keep retained backups.
- **Behaviour change:** waking a custom image that lacks the restore endpoint now reports a recovery failure. Before, it skipped the restore silently. The owner can explicitly consent with `forceFreshBoot`.

## #20726 (partial; consolidates #30746): durable backup/restore lifecycle

Refs #20726. This is an umbrella issue and must stay open.

**Unfunded/legacy stop path (#30746).** A bound billing suspension whose current backup cannot be captured used to refuse the stop, so unpaid compute kept running. It now stops the exact observed runtime **in place**:

- The restart policy is disabled and the container is stopped by its immutable ID. It is never removed.
- `agent_sandboxes.retained_runtime` (migration 0478) records the exact runtime and its private ingress. While it is set:
  - node deprovisioning counts the row;
  - the orphan reaper sees a live placement;
  - provision, restore and sleep refuse to replace the runtime.
- `executeResume` restarts that same container in place after billing settles, publishes ingress only after readiness, and clears the marker.
- A missing or replaced runtime is a visible failure, never a fresh boot.

**#30820 (manifest-v3 restore candidates) was not ported.**
- Its Agent-side substrate was deleted from `develop` in `dce4782e17` ("updates", 2026-09-23). That is roughly 14k lines: the `agent-backup-restore-v3-candidate-{fs,records,file-set,...}` and `agent-backup-v2-capture` modules.
- Its only behavioural test (`agent-backup-restore-v3-materialization.pglite.test.ts`) imports those modules.
- The cloud-side join/seal hunks apply with a trivial import-path fix, but they would land as dormant code with no materializer and no runnable test. I left them out.
- The intent (never route before a verified restore, never silently boot empty) is delivered on the production restore path by the #30697 commit.
- **Owner decision needed:** whether the v3 Agent materializer is coming back.

**Still needs live staging or further work for #20726:**
- A staging drill on a real Docker node for the retained stop. It must cover:
  - `docker update --restart=no`, then stop, then inspect;
  - mounts and secrets retained;
  - node deprovisioning blocked;
  - exact restart.
- Physical provider evidence for the #29678 exact cleanup. The same-name successor survives, driven through the real Docker identity resolver over SSH.
- The #20732 explicit restore (materialize, commit generation, boot/attest, route CAS) and the v3 decision above.
- #24594 node-loss failover, #24601 incremental chains / multipart publication, #24407 fair admission, #31270 heartbeat-vs-backup, #31325 restore memory budget, #31334/#31355 drain before cutover.
- A staging backup, wipe, restore, boot and remembered-fact drill with measured RPO/RTO (artifacts A–E).

## #30702: automatically resume billing-suspended agents when funding returns

Fixes #30702

The provisioning worker reconciles one cursor page per cycle, using the existing stop intents and provisioning jobs. No new scheduler is added.

- **Discovery** selects provider-confirmed `billing_request` stops that meet all of:
  - the intent is still the agent's newest intent;
  - the agent is still `stopped`, not deleting, not replacement-fenced and unowned;
  - no exclusive lifecycle job was created after the stop;
  - the organization admits paid work.
- **Admission** re-reads funding through the canonical credit gate (cash plus eligible subscription allowance). It re-verifies the stop authority under the agent lifecycle lock in the enqueue transaction. Repeated scans and concurrent refills therefore reuse one `agent_resume` job.
- **Execution** rechecks authority and funding before any effect. A later user stop, a deletion or lapsed funding completes the job as an explicit `skipped` instead of resuming.
- **Crash repair:** a crash between a credit commit and the enqueue is repaired by the next page.
- **Resume paths:** retained runtimes restart in place; removed containers re-provision through the fail-closed restore from #30697.
- **Remaining:** live verification of the final database, runtime and provider state before production rollout.

## #25146 (server-side mechanism): reversible Dedicated-to-Shared fallback

Refs #25146

- **Most conservative withdrawal policy.** Dedicated access is withdrawn only when both hold:
  - the runtime is stopped by a provider-confirmed billing stop that is still its latest lifecycle decision;
  - the organization is **unfunded now**.

  Transient, unknown, user-initiated and funded states keep Dedicated authority with the existing honest unavailability.
- **Durable authority:** `personal_dedicated_fallbacks` (migration 0480) holds one active interval per account. Each interval has a monotonic generation, the withdrawing stop intent, and a new scoped Shared journal (`fallback:<uuid>`). A later withdrawal opens a new generation and never reopens an older interval.
- **Memory boundary:** Shared answers in the scoped journal and never in the canonical Dedicated/pre-upgrade room. Responses carry a typed `accountState`: access, reason, `dedicatedMemory: "unavailable"`, generation, and the billing recovery path. It contains no payment details.
- **Recovery:** once Dedicated runs again, both consumers close the interval before routing returns.
- **One decision for both consumers:** personal message delivery and the personal-shared messages route use the same authority decision. Group turns are unchanged.
- **Remaining (product decisions or live evidence):**
  - importing the Shared interval into Dedicated on recovery;
  - a model-facing account provider;
  - signed, expiring recovery links;
  - notice dedupe;
  - isolating Shared long-term memory beyond conversation history;
  - a policy for trial, grace, dunning and cancel-at-period-end;
  - live Stripe sandbox and connector certification with desktop/mobile evidence.

## #22930 (clearly-correct slice only): payment reversal entitlement

Refs #22930. The owner decision is still required, so this does not close the issue.

**Implemented:**
- A dispute that closes **lost** (`charge.dispute.closed`, `status: "lost"`) records a durable, idempotent `organization_payment_reversal_holds` row (migration 0479). The row is attributed through the organization credit grant that the charge funded.
- While a hold is active:
  - the canonical agent credit gate denies new paid admission (create, provision, resume, wake, upgrade) regardless of balance;
  - automatic resume (#30702) skips the organization.
- A hold ends only through an explicit, audited release (`releasePaymentReversalHold`, with actor and reason required).
- Won disputes, open disputes, refunds and charges without a credit grant never create a hold.
- The existing clawback on `funds_withdrawn` is untouched.

**Open decision points for the owner (Decision A/B/C and the eight fields):**
1. Should refunds, partial refunds or `funds_withdrawn` (before a dispute is decided) also hold, or only a lost dispute (current behaviour)?
2. Is the unrecovered clawback shortfall (`unrecovered_clawback_usd`) authoritative debt, written off, or a separate state?
3. Which gates beyond agent admission should a hold block: inference, storage, domains, API keys, apps, containers?
4. What happens to already-running billable resources during a hold (continue, suspend, stop), with what exact timing, and who pays? Nothing is stopped today.
5. Which capabilities stay available during a hold (sign-in, billing history, export, deletion)?
6. Restoration: automatic after repayment or a won re-dispute, or a reviewed release (current behaviour)? Who may release?
7. UI states, notifications, receipts and support/audit requirements (#22966).
8. Interaction with subscription dunning states.
9. Creator payouts (#23022) are moot, since mini-app/creator payouts were removed.

3d:T1abc,Fixes and ports across the connector, search, MCP and wallet plugins. One commit per issue.

## #32027: outbound `data:` and media-store attachments (Discord, Telegram, Slack)
Fixes #32027. Ported from closed #32032 (credit @hermesagent270-commits).

- Adds `resolveOutboundAttachmentBytes` to core. It handles each kind of URL as follows:
  - `data:` URLs are decoded in process, with a size cap.
  - Canonical `/api/media/...` handles use the runtime's local fetch.
  - http(s) URLs go through the SSRF-guarded fetcher.
  - Filesystem paths and other schemes are rejected without reading disk.
- Telegram, Discord and Slack now upload the resolved bytes.
- Logs and delivery evidence carry only a scheme/host/path summary. The base64 payload no longer appears in the Telegram error.
- If a Telegram attachment fails, the text reply is still sent.
- Behavior change: Telegram no longer reads local file paths passed as attachment URLs. Treating a model-supplied path as a file on disk was the bug.

## #32026: bounded Discord attachment cache
Fixes #32026. Ported from #32032.

- The cache is now keyed by the stable attachment id instead of the signed CDN URL.
- It holds at most 64 entries (LRU) with a 2-minute TTL.
- Extracted transcript text is no longer kept in the cache.

## #31464: per-account Discord disconnect (partial, `Refs`)
`deleteAccount` used to be a no-op. It now works as follows:

1. **Persist a disabled policy first.** It goes in the runtime cache, keyed by account id and a SHA-256 fingerprint of the token. The token itself is never stored.
2. **Disconnect the account.** `DiscordService.disconnectAccount`:
   - blocks new messages for that account only;
   - drains its admitted turns, within the existing shutdown-drain timeout;
   - tears down its client, login retry, debouncer, managers and audio sinks.
3. **Siblings are unaffected.** Other accounts keep running, and the default alias moves to a remaining account.
4. **Failures are retryable.** A teardown failure throws before the manager deletes its row, and a retry repeats the teardown.
5. **Restart honors the policy.** After a restart, the service skips the disabled token and the provider stops listing it. Configuring a new token under the same id counts as a replacement and runs normally.

Remaining, outside this plugin:
- Removing the token from character settings or env. The host's settings owner has to do this; the plugin cannot write it.
- The issue's gating of all-channel onboarding completion, which belongs to the onboarding flow.

## #31789: typed keyless web-search failures (+ #31785 SSE binding)
Fixes #31789.

- `searchKeylessWeb` now returns `undefined` only when a search succeeds with zero hits.
- Every provider failure throws `KeylessWebSearchUnavailableError`, with:
  - a reason: `rate_limited`, `http_error`, `timeout`, `network`, `aborted`, `malformed_response`, `response_too_large`, `unsupported_content_type` or `provider_error`;
  - the HTTP status and `Retry-After`;
  - no query text or provider payload in the message.
- The search takes a caller `AbortSignal` and never dispatches after cancellation.
- The MCP response parser now handles SSE strictly (#31785):
  - it parses complete SSE events, including multi-line and unspaced `data:` fields;
  - it ignores notifications and responses to other request ids;
  - identical replays are collapsed, and conflicting or missing responses are rejected.
- Callers updated: the agent `WEB_SEARCH` action, the edge runner, coding-tools and the cloud keyless search. A provider outage is now reported as unavailable, not as "no results" / `no_match`.

## #29650: platform-filtered MCP tool discovery
Fixes #29650.

- `Tier2ToolIndex.search` now ranks the whole index, filters by platform, and only then applies offset and limit.
- It returns the total match count, and `SEARCH_ACTIONS` reports `totalMatches` and `hasMore`.

## #29930: EVM relative swap sizing (money path)
Fixes #29930. Reworked from conflicting #29933 (credit @agent-cortex).

- Half / max / percent are now sized from the balance of the token being sold:
  - **ERC-20:** the token's on-chain `balanceOf` and `decimals`. Symbols resolve through Li.Fi, the same way `SwapAction` does. `max` spends the full balance.
  - **Native asset:** the native balance, and `max` keeps the 10% gas reserve.
- All math uses bigint raw units, so large, dust and high-precision balances are never rounded through floats.
- A failed balance read, an unconfigured chain or a zero result throws `INVALID_PARAMS`. The code never falls back to another token's balance.

## #31110: Solana RPC failure shown as $0
Fixes #31110. Ported from conflicting #31127 (credit @hermesagent270-commits).

- The token-account and SOL balance reads now throw `ElizaError`:
  - `SOLANA_RPC_UNAVAILABLE` for an RPC failure;
  - `SOLANA_RPC_RATE_LIMITED` after 3 backed-off attempts on 429. The old code retried 429 without limit.
- `updateWalletData` no longer caches an empty portfolio when a read fails, so the last known value stays.
- A failed first refresh no longer makes the configured wallet look absent.
- The wallet routes answer 503 with the typed code.

## #30151: web wake word matched as a substring
Fixes #30151. Ported from closed #30152 (credit @agent-cortex).

- The trigger must now be a standalone token, so "elizabeth" no longer fires "eliza".
- The boundary rule is relaxed for scripts written without spaces (CJK, Thai and similar).

3e:T1dde,Two issues, one commit each. Both are partial: the code-completable work is here, and what still needs staging or production is listed under each issue. The commits use `Refs`, so merging does not auto-close either issue.

## #30806: subscriber admission off the synchronous funding path (partial)
Refs #30806

Before this change, every subscription-funded Worker request ran `reserveSynchronously(params, true)` before provider dispatch. That call is a locked primary transaction that allocates allowance and can also debit purchased credit. After this change, token-priced subscriber requests on the Worker take the Durable Object lease lane:

- **Admission:** reads funding capacity (purchased credit plus spendable allowance) inside the existing policy transaction and reuses that transaction's balance, revision and clock. It then leases that capacity in the organization's admission Durable Object, which serializes every in-flight estimate and runs the fused credential check.
- **After the provider responds:** `fundSubscriberInferenceCharge` funds the exact charge, allowance first, in one primary transaction. That transaction also reads the post-accounting capacity, so settling the gate needs no balance readback.
- **Alarm recovery:** replays the same `inference-gate:<requestId>` funding key, so a lease recovered after a lost settlement is never funded twice.
- **Zero-collected replay:** settlement now persists a durable zero-collected receipt (migration `0493`) while retaining the actual cost. Replaying an earlier unfunded turn cannot charge newly added funds or substitute a later lease estimate. Real database regression coverage exercises the replay boundary.

**How overspend is prevented**
- **Revision:** migration `0491` advances `organizations.balance_revision` on every platform allowance transaction. Allowance-only spend, grants, releases and expiry therefore all look newer to the gate.
- **Ceiling view:** the gate ledger records whether its ceiling is credit only or funding capacity (`balanceView: "funding"`).
  - At an equal revision, the funding view replaces a credit-only observation.
  - A newer revision always wins.
  - Two observations of the same view can only lower the ceiling.
  - A lease must carry the funding view exactly when its recovery accounting is `subscription_funding`.
  - The view field is additive, so a rollback reads the ledger conservatively.
- **Settlement:** caps the charge at the capacity available under the organization lock. Any remainder is reported as `uncollected_overage` and is never overdrawn, which matches the synchronous lane's policy.

**Unchanged:** affiliate-marked, flat-priced (pending video) and non-Worker subscriber requests keep the synchronous reservation.

**Folded micro-issues**
- **#30724 (done):** `requireInferenceApiKeyWithOrg` now resolves key, user and organization with one primary statement. Rejection classes, messages and order are unchanged. Timing is now a single `auth_identity` hop, and the latency script is updated to match.
- **#30715 (done):** as described above.
- **#30860, #30865, #30718, #30722 (already fixed on develop):**
  - #30860: the skip-mode memo write is gated in `steward-client.ts`.
  - #30865: the affiliate settlement publishes its transaction's balance and revision.
  - #30718: the policy statement already reads balance and revision.
  - #30722: the aged deferred refresh landed in #32834.
- **#30796 (remaining):** enabling `INFERENCE_STRONG_REVOCATION_ENABLED` in production needs the staging allow/deny/revoke canary and a normal release. This PR does not change it.

**Remaining**
- The policy-read and dispatch-policy transactions still run before dispatch. They were already there, so the zero pre-dispatch DB goal is still not met.
- Live Worker-to-Cerebras certification and the traces for the exact deployed revision.
- #30796, above.

## #22552: first-turn warming 503s (partial)
Refs #22552

Partial work landed in #32834, which added the session-start prewarm and the keepwarm conversation-object leg for Personal Shared rooms. This PR adds three things.

**Bounded join instead of an immediate warming 503**
- The conversation Durable Object's history hydration, the linked-character cache and the combined admission snapshot now wait up to 4s for their in-flight hydration before failing.
- Hydration that is slower than that, or that fails, still fails closed with the same retryable warming signal. The hydration keeps running under `waitUntil`.

**Keep-warm for rowless Personal Shared identities**
- A canonical personal turn records its verified owner in its conversation object. A forged projection, whose id is not derived from its account, is ignored.
- The room prewarm reports that organization, and the cron then warms the organization's rate-limit gate once per sweep. The namespaced id is never sent to a UUID lookup.

**Terminal transcript (#27685)**
- The startup/recovery restore (`hydrateInitialConversation`) wrote its server snapshot straight into the transcript, bypassing the local-turn overlay.
- A restore that landed while a send was absorbing warming retries evicted the pending turn. The next overlay capture then retired that turn for good, so the receipt had nothing left to re-anchor.
- The restore now merges through the same overlay reconciliation that history loads use. A regression test that uses the real `useChatSend`, `useDataLoaders` and `hydrateInitialConversation` reproduced the staging signature before the fix.

**Remaining**
- The 503-rate and time-to-first-reply re-measurement, with a sample size stated in advance.
- The canonical deployed staging run showing the anchored terminal turn.
- #27685 receipt and reconciliation counters: the app exposes no signal to count from yet.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
40:T14ff,## Fixes #25146: reversible, entitlement-driven Dedicated-to-Shared recovery

Builds on the fallback interval that already landed on develop (`personal_dedicated_fallbacks`, migration 0480, which only handled credit-funded billing stops). This PR turns it into one revision-fenced state machine on the existing tier/route authority, driven by paid-plan status:

`dedicated_active -> fallback_pending -> shared_active -> recovery_pending -> recovered (dedicated_active)`

**Policy (owner-authorized).** Dedicated is withdrawn when the org's Plus/Pro plan lapses. That means `past_due`/`unpaid` after the dunning grace period, or `canceled`, including cancel-at-period-end once the cancellation is confirmed. The decision is read from the `organization_entitlements` projection and the exact subscription revision it was built from. These cases never withdraw:
- an active plan whose allowance is spent
- `grace`
- a deadline that passed without a confirmed lifecycle event (webhook lag)
- an `incomplete_expired` checkout

The existing confirmed-unfunded billing-stop path still applies. Payment-reversal holds are left to #22930.

**Durable, fenced transitions (migration `0490`, journal updated; 0485–0489 left free).** Every transition:
- runs under the account advisory lock
- re-reads the projection inside the transaction
- compare-and-swaps a row `revision`
- ignores projection revisions older than the one already applied

So concurrent webhooks, reconciler pages and connector turns converge on one interval and one set of effects.

**Container preserved, same id restored.**
- A live Dedicated runtime is stopped through the lifecycle queue inside the transition transaction. The state is captured and the runtime is never deleted. `retain_until` is set to now + 30 days and shown to the user.
- When entitlement returns, a resume is admitted for the same agent id, only after the stop has settled and only once.
- Credit-driven automatic resume (#30702) is fenced while a plan withdrawal is active.
- The provisioning worker runs an entitlement reconcile phase before billing resume, so the paid runtime stops without waiting for a connector turn.

**Zero Dedicated memory access in Shared.**
- Each withdrawal generation gets a new scoped journal (`fallback:<uuid>`).
- Fallback turns never read agent-scoped facts, which are shared with Dedicated. Recall and history are already pinned to the journal room.
- A typed minimal account-state provider (`state`, `reason`, `dedicatedRetainedUntil`, `recoveryAction` of `restore_subscription` or `add_credits` at `/cloud/billing`) is resolved on the server. It is validated exactly at the Durable Object boundary, and any other shape gets a 400. It is rendered into the model context and tells the model to say plainly that Dedicated memory is unavailable. It contains no payment details.

**Recovery.** Before routing returns to Dedicated, the complete journal is imported into Dedicated, idempotently by source message id. The switch is committed with a receipt after a fresh entitlement check. If the import fails, the turn returns a retryable 503 and the interval stays `recovery_pending`.

### Verification
- `bun test src/lib/services/__tests__/billing-suspension-resume.pglite.test.ts` (cloud-shared): 8 pass / 0 fail. This is real PGlite plus the real `ProvisioningJobService` queue. It covers:
  - the policy table
  - concurrent withdrawal converging on one interval and one stop
  - no delete jobs
  - the credit auto-resume fence (confirmed to fail with the fence removed)
  - a stale or reordered projection being ignored
  - a single resume of the same agent id
  - the receipt-backed route commit
  - a new generation after the plan ends
  - a refused final commit when entitlement is lost mid-recovery
  - the reconciler path without a connector turn
  - the updated legacy credit path
- `bun test __tests__/shared-runtime-cutover-reminder.miniflare.test.ts` (cloud-api, Workerd): 2 pass. The new assertions show that a fallback journal turn is admitted while the canonical room is cutover-sealed, loads none of the canonical history, receives the exact account state, and that malformed state is rejected.
- `tsc --noEmit` for cloud-shared and cloud-api: clean. `check:router-contract` and `check:worker-bundle`: pass.
- Biome on cloud-shared, cloud-api and the daemon: no errors (existing warnings only).
- `check-migration-prefix-order`: pass.
- `scripts/admin/daemons/provisioning-worker-env-reconcile.test.ts`: 3 failures in the bash workflow-block env tests. These do not touch this change and are macOS/environment related.

### Remaining (needs staging, owner or other trackers)
- A live signed Stripe sandbox lifecycle run, with real Dedicated health/route readback and live-model fallback/recovery trajectories.
- Owner confirmation of the 30-day retention value. Nothing deletes the agent after it.

## #32016: agent export secrets (Fixes #32016)
The maintainer closed #32028 because a migration bundle is meant to carry credentials, and asked for a secrets-free export to be **opt-in** instead. So this PR does that:
- The default bundle still carries `secrets` / `settings.secrets`, protected by the bundle password. The module header now says so, where it used to imply they were excluded.
- `POST /api/agent/export` accepts `excludeSecrets: true` (core contract, route, UI client). That option strips both containers from the agent record and from the runtime character. A non-object `settings` gets a typed `AGENT_EXPORT_INVALID_SETTINGS` error.
- Import is unchanged. A default bundle restores the credentials it carries.
- Test: `test/agent-transfer-secrets.test.ts` runs on real PGlite. It checks that a default bundle round-trips `settings.secrets`, and that a credential-free bundle has no secret in the decrypted payload or on the imported agent.

## #31952: atomic content-addressed media writer (Fixes #31952)
Ported from #31973 and cut down as the maintainer asked:
- Persist, restore and the pin ledger now share one write-temp-then-rename helper.
- If a file already exists under a content hash but its size differs from the payload, it is rewritten.
- Dropped from the original PR: the extra fsync, the GC sweep and the logging.

- Not done: the #30019 sub-item (the plugin-meetings duplicate writer) is outside this PR's scope.

## #31953: WebSocket fan-out backpressure (Fixes #31953)
Ported from #31974.
- A peer's queued bytes are the transport buffer plus frames waiting on host admission.
- A peer above the soft limit keeps getting every frame for a grace period. If it still hasn't drained, it is terminated. Above the hard limit it is terminated at once.
- Frames are never skipped on a live socket. The client sees the termination and replays from the event buffer when it reconnects.
- A ping/pong sweep prunes peers that stop answering.
- Note for review: #31974 was closed because of a general concern about caps. This version never drops frames silently; the only failure mode is an observable disconnect followed by replay.
- Test: `test/event-hub-backpressure.test.ts` uses a real `ws` server and clients. A stalled peer is bounded and terminated while a healthy peer gets all 2000 frames. A peer that stops answering pings is pruned.

## #31239: trade-mode write failure (Fixes #31239)
Ported from #31259.
- A failed `saveElizaConfig` restores the previous in-memory shape, including removing a `features` object or mode key the handler itself created.
- The route then answers a structured 500 instead of `ok: true`.
- Test: `test/trade-mode-persistence-http.test.ts` drives the handler over loopback HTTP with the real config writer, with the config path blocked by a directory.

## #31881: owner-private audience renewal during long turns (Fixes #31881)
The 5-minute TTL is unchanged. What's new:
- **Turn lease.** The message turn (plugin-assistant `turn-lifetime.ts`) opens a lease with `beginTrustedDeliveryAudienceTurn`. The lease is bound to the runtime, to this turn's evidence binding, and to its merged cancellation signal. It closes when processing settles.
- **Renewal.** Expired evidence is re-derived from current authority:
  - Canonical rooms re-read the room kind, the owner and the full participant census.
  - API evidence renews only through a host-supplied fresh principal check.
- **What renewal must match.** It succeeds only if actor, agent, room, destination kind, owner and census are all unchanged. Sensitive-disclosure bookkeeping carries over.
- **When renewal is refused.** After completion or cancellation, for a different runtime, and for evidence that was already expired when the turn began.
- **Where it runs.** Inside revalidation, before the planner's exposure gates, and before owner-gated tool execution.
- Test: `packages/core/src/security/trusted-delivery-audience.renewal.test.ts` (14 cases) advances the clock past the TTL. It covers a long turn succeeding and each invalidation case.
- Not done:
  - `chat-routes.ts` does not yet pass `revalidatePrincipal` to `attestAuthenticatedApiDeliveryAudience`, so authenticated-API turns still expire at 5 minutes. That needs a host-side fresh session or token check.
  - The `live-note-to-calendar-stale-source` scenario was not re-run (needs a live provider).

43:T105f,## Refs #25146: reversible Dedicated to Shared fallback, follow-up to #32856

#32856 added the fallback authority but only the connector paths used it. This PR covers the three code items that were left.

**1. Direct chat routes use the entitlement route.** A new module, `personal-direct-chat-route.ts`, gives every direct entry point for the `personal:` identity the same decision the connector paths already get from `resolvePersonalDedicatedRoute`. The entry points are:
- `GET /api/v1/eliza/personal`
- REST `conversations` list/create and `conversations/:id/messages` GET/POST
- `conversations/:id/messages/stream`
- `agents/:id/stream`
- `agents/:id/bridge` (`message.send`)

How each state is handled:
- **Access withdrawn:** the canonical conversation id is served from the interval's scoped journal, with the typed account-state provider. That provider now also reaches streaming, because `coordinateSharedStream` forwards `trustedAccountState`. Any other room returns 404, so the canonical, Dedicated and pre-upgrade history is never read.
- **Dedicated owns the conversation:** Shared surfaces return a typed 409 `personal_eliza_dedicated` with `activeAgentId`. Before, only the sealed canonical room refused; other room ids got through.
- **Recovery:** the journal is reconciled into the same Dedicated agent id before `/personal` or a surface hands that id back. If the coordinator is missing, the response is a retryable 503 `dedicated_reconciling`.

**2. Signed, expiring recovery links.** The pay action (`recoveryAction.link`) now carries an ES256 JWS. It is signed with the existing Cloud JWKS key (`lib/auth/jwks`), bound to org, user, interval id, generation and action kind, and expires after 72h. The public route `GET /api/v1/eliza/personal/recovery/:token` verifies the token and checks that the interval still exists. It then redirects to `/cloud/billing?organizationId=…&action=…`. The link carries no payment details and grants nothing: the billing page still requires sign-in. An expired link returns 410 and a bad token returns 400. The DO boundary parser admits only a well-formed resolver URL (no query, hash or credentials).

If the signing key is not configured, the link is left out and the gap is logged at error level. The `/cloud/billing` path still works.

**3. Group and shared-gateway traffic.** `resolvePersonalDedicatedTrafficAccess` refuses a withdrawn personal Dedicated with the typed state in four places:
- the connector gateway (`agent-gateway-router`, new reason `dedicated_access_withdrawn`)
- the Dedicated bridge dispatch in `agents/:id/bridge`
- the container control-plane `bridge`/`stream`
- group turns in `personal-shared/messages`

Group turns get a neutral `group_dedicated_access_paused` reply that does not disclose the owner's billing state. Once the owner's access is back, a group turn also triggers the recovery reconcile. Agents that are not cut-over personal Dedicated agents are unaffected.

It checks room mapping, the 404 for foreign rooms, gateway/group refusal, and link sign, resolve, tamper and expiry.

44:T2326,This PR covers two areas: Cloud front-door sign-out and session coherence (#29918), and the iOS native Cloud credential lifecycle (#16420). Both issues remain partial because their remaining work needs a staging run or a physical device.

## #29918: coherent sign-in, sign-out and recovery (partial)
Refs #29918

#32834 already re-landed the OAuth launch-origin fix and the bootstrap recovery-screen accessibility. This PR re-lands the remaining front-door pieces of the closed draft #30920 (credit: standujar), sized to what current develop can carry.

**`/join` sign-out now reaches a signed-out state.** The recorded failure was a sign-out that bounced back through `/login` into `/join` and restarted Dedicated setup. Two client paths could restore the session that had just ended:
- **Refresh race:** `StewardProviderRuntime`'s refresh-ahead loop wrote a rotated token unconditionally. A refresh that was in flight during sign-out therefore republished the session after it had been cleared. It now uses `replaceStoredStewardTokenIfCurrent`, a compare-and-swap against the token the refresh started from. A regression test fails without this change.
- **Surviving refresh cookie:** the login page silently restored a refresh cookie that survived sign-out. It no longer does this while the origin's explicit sign-out marker is set. The next explicit sign-in still clears the marker: through the login section, the SSO exchange, or, new here, a completed magic link.
- `JoinPage` now guards sign-out with a ref, so one activation sends exactly one hosted logout.

**Session revalidation after confirmation.** A join attempt is now bound to the account that started it. If the user signs out, switches account, or another tab signs in during the Dedicated quote or adoption review, the attempt is aborted before the billable POST and the user gets a retryable error. A token rotation for the same subject does not abort the attempt.

**Email completion.** Ordinary callback and cross-tab email completion use neutral Continue copy; only the app-authorization destination advertises app authorization.

**Bootstrap recovery screen.** The session is now persisted before it is published. A storage failure shows a recoverable error; before, it left the screen stuck on "submitting" with an unhandled rejection.

**#27241 (consolidated leaf):** phone and email/passkey validation messages now:
- appear next to the field that failed;
- set `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` on that field;
- keep `role=alert`;
- move focus to the invalid input.

| #19171 | Fixed in code. `describeBackendFailure`/`BACKEND_NOT_FOUND` exists in `startup-phase-poll.ts`, and the staging API mounts were already fixed. Remaining: a credentialed staging run from a fresh magic link through `/auth/bridge` to `/join` and a reload. |
| #19214 | **Not fixed.** The app host still hands off a full page to the auth host (`redirectToSsoBridge` → `staging.eliza.app/login`). Moving passwordless login onto the app origin is an architecture change that needs a product decision. |

| #27241 | Fixed here. The empty-field "no OTP request" check is covered by the existing tests. |

## #16420: iOS native Cloud authentication (partial)
Refs #16420

This builds on the existing `ElizaCloudAuthSessionPlugin`, which uses ASWebAuthenticationSession with S256 PKCE and the claimed HTTPS callback `https://eliza.app/auth/callback`, and on the shared mobile PKCE client, which calls `app-auth/mobile/{config,token,ack}`.

- **Inactive until ACK:** the exchanged secret is staged in its own Keychain slot. The new secure-store key `session.cloud_mobile_pending` is added to the Swift, Kotlin and TS allowlists. The secret is promoted into the Keychain-backed Steward token store only after the server ACK. If the ACK response fails or is lost, the staged secret is retained for exact-key revocation: the server may already have committed the ACK. The prior active session remains untouched.
- **Exact recovery:** the ACK is recorded on the staged record before promotion. At launch, and before a new sign-in:
  - an acknowledged staged credential is promoted exactly once, without opening a second browser;
  - an unacknowledged one is revoked with its exact key before its local record is removed; a failed revoke preserves the record for retry.
- **Revocation:** sign-out and disconnect revoke any staged credential before the active credential or SSO session. Each exact-key `DELETE /api/v1/api-keys/current` must succeed before its local copy is removed. This prevents a staged credential left by failed activation from restoring a session after sign-out. Failed revocation preserves the corresponding credential for retry.
- **#30853 (consolidated):**
  - Native device-code sign-in now opens the owned Capacitor Browser instead of a renderer popup.
  - If persisting a claimed one-time CLI token fails, the client retries from memory instead of polling the consumed session again. A regression test fails without this change.
- **#28888 (consolidated):** the code-side items are already on develop:
  - idempotent callback ACK;
  - typed terminal failures that survive a cleanup failure;
  - removal of the stripped Android-only shell.

  Its remaining acceptance is device evidence (see below).

**Remaining (needs a device, signing and a deploy):**
- Clean-install runs on an iOS device and an Android device, including sign-in, cancel, cold relaunch, revocation, and the recovery paths.
- AASA/`webcredentials` verification for the signed team and bundle.
- Enabling the mobile config routes in staging and production.
- Cloud-only defaults for store builds.
- A product decision on iOS 16.0–17.4, which keeps the device-code fallback.

45:T218a,## #22930: billing hold after an underfunding refund or chargeback (Decision A)

Refs #22930

The owner approved option A. This PR builds on the existing clawback mechanics in `credits.ts` and `stripe-event.ts`, and on the `organization_payment_reversal_holds` table from migration 0479. It does not add a second hold model; migration **0492** extends that table.

- **Server-owned hold.** Sometimes a refund or dispute clawback cannot take the full reversed amount from the balance, leaving an unrecovered shortfall. In that case `clawbackCredits` inserts a `reversal_shortfall` hold **in the same SQL statement** as the clawback. That means a committed shortfall always has its hold. The hold records `shortfall_usd` and `outstanding_usd` and links to the clawback transaction.
- **Fail-closed admission.** While a hold is active, paid admission is refused with a typed `BillingHoldActiveError` (`code: billing_hold_active`). It extends `InsufficientCreditsError`, so every paid route that already maps a funding denial to HTTP 402 returns 402 for it. The hold is checked in these places:
  - inference admission (`admitOrganizationInference`, inside the policy transaction);
  - pre-spend reservations (`reserveCredits`, `reserveFlatUsageCredits`, `reserveAllowanceEligibleCredits`), which also block Plus/Pro allowance funding;
  - new containers (`createWithQuotaAndOptionalProjectIntent`, under the org lock);
  - Dedicated create, provision, resume, wake and upgrade (the existing `checkAgentCreditGate`, whose 402 body now carries `billing_hold_active` and `outstandingUsd`);
  - automatic resume.
- **Clearing the hold.**
  - Repayment: a Stripe top-up settles outstanding shortfalls oldest-first before anything else, recorded as a ledger debit. A hold is released once it is covered. The pay action also applies funds already in the balance.
  - `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`: releases the hold for that clawback, and returns any repayment already made toward it as a separate refund row that is not tagged with the payment intent, so later refund tallies stay correct.
  - Operator release still works (now keyed by hold id).
- **Lost disputes.** `charge.dispute.closed` with status lost no longer places a separate hold. The shortfall hold from `funds_withdrawn` already covers what is owed. Migration 0492 moves any still-owed legacy lost-dispute shortfall into a shortfall hold before it releases the old `chargeback_lost` holds.
- **API and UI.**
  - `GET /api/v1/billing/hold` returns a typed `{status: "clear"} | {status: "held", outstandingUsd, holds, payAction}`.
  - `POST /api/v1/billing/hold` (billing manager session only) applies the current balance.
  - The Billing tab shows a hold banner with **Pay $X**, which prefills the card top-up, and **Apply current balance**.

| Policy field | Decision |
|---|---|
| Trigger | Any refund (including partial, cumulative) or `funds_withdrawn` clawback that leaves `unrecovered_clawback_usd > 0`. A fully recovered reversal never holds. |
| Shortfall | Authoritative debt equal to the unrecovered amount. Credits that land later repay it first. |
| Blocked while held | Inference (credit- and allowance-funded), media/search/voice pre-spend, new containers, Dedicated create/provision/resume/wake/upgrade, automatic resume. |
| Running resources | Not force-stopped. They keep settling against existing funding and follow the existing unfunded-stop lifecycle. The credit balance is 0 while held. |
| Still available | Sign-in, billing history, reads/exports, cancellation/deletion, top-ups. |
| Restoration | Automatic once repayment covers the outstanding amount or Stripe reinstates the dispute. Operator release is kept for write-offs. |
| UI | Typed hold state, owed amount and pay action on the Billing tab. |
| Scope | Forward-only: shortfalls recorded before this deploy are not backfilled, except legacy lost-dispute shortfalls. |

46:T2384,Android native runtime fixes: bionic output limits, musl loader staging, JNI staging, Notes on Android, TalkMode cancellation, cloud reconnect cancellation, and pairing contract tests. One commit per issue.

Disk on the build host was critically low, so no Gradle, AOSP, APK or native builds were run. Device acceptance is listed per issue under "Remaining".

## #32412: Android bridge injects a 256-token cap
Fixes #32412

`plugins/plugin-native-inference/src/mobile-device-bridge-bootstrap.ts` sent `maxTokens: lane.maxTokens ?? 256` to the bionic host, so uncapped chat turns failed with `MODEL_OUTPUT_INCOMPLETE` after 256 tokens. An omitted limit now stays omitted, so the host owns the decode boundary (`ElizaBionicInferenceServer` defaults to `residentStreamGenerationBoundary()`). Explicit limits are still forwarded. The request guard reserves output only for an explicit limit, both here and on the device-bridge path that used `?? 256`. The host's `incomplete` receipt now fails the turn even when no limit was supplied, matching `BionicHostLoader`.

- New `mobile-device-bridge-bootstrap.output-limit.test.ts` drives the registered TEXT handler over the real framed transport (buffered and streaming). It covers: uncapped output over 256 tokens, the full prompt preserved, an explicit limit forwarded and enforced, and native exhaustion rejected when uncapped (buffered and streaming).
- `vitest run` (output-limit, stream-step, gating): 13/13 pass. The full package suite `vitest run --config vitest.config.ts`: 45 files, 323 pass, 2 skipped. Package `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.build.json`: exit 0.
- Remaining: rebuild the APK and run a real full-turn chat longer than 256 tokens on a device.

## #32511: restaging overwrites the real musl loader
Fixes #32511

`stageSeccompShimForAbi` used a 200 KiB size heuristic, but the ARM64 Zig wrapper is about 1 MiB. The staged file is now classified by identity:
- **Wrapper:** matches the cached wrapper bytes, or is a static ELF `ET_EXEC`.
- **musl loader:** an ELF `ET_DYN`.
- **Anything else:** fails closed.

`.real` is verified to be the loader after every run.

- New tests in `scripts/stage-android-agent.test.ts`. They stage three times with a 1,064,168-byte ET_EXEC wrapper and check that the `.real` SHA-256 is unchanged and that the shim library is staged. They check that an outdated wrapper is replaced without touching `.real`. They also check that a wrapper sitting at `.real`, or a non-ELF candidate, is refused.
- `node --test --test-name-pattern=SIGSYS scripts/stage-android-agent.test.ts`: 4/4 pass.
- Remaining: on-device execution of the restaged runtime.

## #32349: keep JNI staging separate from Bun musl assets
Fixes #32349

`copyForkLlamaLib` no longer discovers `assets/agent/<abi>` (Bun musl) or the musl mtp compile cache. JNI lib dirs come only from the explicit `eliza.mtp.android.libdir[.<abi>]` / `ELIZA_MTP_ANDROID_LIBDIR[_<ABI>]` overrides or the NDK-staged `jniLibs` slice. The elizavoice probe resolver got the same treatment.

arm64-v8a and x86_64 (the ABIs the JNI CMake target links) now both fail before any native build. Every missing-slice error names `node packages/app/scripts/stage-elizavoice-lib.ts --abi <abi>`. The generator (`gradle-patches.ts`) and the checked-in `build.gradle` are kept in sync. Cloud skip, riscv64 optional staging, and `assertBionicInferenceLibrary` are unchanged.

- New test in `scripts/run-mobile-build-android-app-actions.test.ts` asserts the same contract on the generator output and on the checked-in template.
- `node --test --test-name-pattern="fork llama|JNI staging" …`: 2/2 pass.
- Remaining: a Gradle build with musl assets and bionic slices both present, then the artifact audit (not run: disk).

## #30725: Notes fails to initialize on Android
Fixes #30725

Android app storage denies `link(2)` with EACCES, so first-boot publication failed. Now, when a hard link is unsupported (EACCES, EPERM, ENOSYS, ENOTSUP or EOPNOTSUPP), the complete document is fsynced inside a private staging directory and renamed onto `<state>.published`. A directory rename cannot replace an existing nonempty publication, so a competing initializer is never overwritten and a partial document is never visible.

The flat file stays authoritative whenever it exists, and every mutation writes it. Other link errors stay fatal. Malformed state still fails with `NOTES_STORE_INVALID_JSON`.

- 5 new real-filesystem tests in `src/__tests__/backend.test.ts`, with only `link` denied:
  - fresh CRUD across restarts
  - a competing publication is preserved
  - concurrent initializers through a symlinked alias share one publication
  - an interrupted staging dir is ignored, and a malformed publication fails loudly
  - a legacy flat file stays authoritative
- Notes `vitest run`: 258/258 pass. `tsc --noEmit`: exit 0. Biome clean.
- Remaining: rebuild the APK and confirm fresh-install Notes readiness and CRUD/restart on a device.

## #30828: TalkMode local speech socket and cancellation
Fixes #30828

- The bionic Kokoro `LocalSocket` is now tracked. `stopSpeakingInternal()` and teardown close it, including during connect, phonemization or the synthesis read.
- A stop during connect is treated as handled, not as an unreachable host, so no fallback voice starts.
- `CancellationException` is rethrown ahead of every generic catch on the speak path: the phonemization wrapper, the local and ElevenLabs handlers, and system TTS. A cancelled call settles once, as interrupted, and never falls back to system speech.
- A replacement `speak()` stops the prior call and joins it before acquiring `pcmTrack`, so only one call owns playback. Sockets are closed before the join, so the join cannot deadlock.
- Verified by compiling the plugin's Kotlin sources with kotlinc 2.2.20 against android-36 and kotlinx-coroutines, using compile-only Capacitor stubs: exit 0, no errors. No Gradle run.
- Remaining: Kotlin/instrumented coverage and a device check of stop, immediate replace and teardown. Playback ordering depends on Android `AudioTrack`/`LocalSocket` and has no JVM seam in this plugin.

## #30844: Android fixes + final build (reconnect cancellation)
Refs #30844

Native bridge logging is already off for Cloud targets, so that item needed no change.

`ConnectionMonitor` now gives each `start()` its own `AbortController`. `stop()` aborts it, which does four things:
- cancels in-flight `heartbeat()` and `provision()` requests (the bridge client now combines a caller signal with its own deadline)
- ends a pending backoff sleep and clears its timer
- makes every continuation, and every synchronous callback that might call `stop()`, drop its results
- stops a slow heartbeat from overlapping into parallel ticks

This ports the stop-ordering tests from closed PR #29942 (credited) and adds tests for request abort, timer cleanup, overlap and restart isolation.

- `vitest run __tests__/reconnect-stop-cancels-inflight.test.ts __tests__/reconnect-exhaustion-report.test.ts`: 15/15 pass. `tsc --noEmit`: exit 0. The full package `vitest run`: 712 pass, 1 fail. The failure is `cloud-container.poll-resilience.test.ts`, which hit a 5s timeout under host load. It is unrelated to this change and passes 11/11 when rerun alone.
- Remaining ("final build", needs device and signing): build the non-canonical Cloud APK without Firebase, confirm Coinbase analytics are absent and the routing audit passes, check the guarded push wrapper, then install and relaunch on a Pixel.

## #31021: Alpha phone pairing (code parts)
Refs #31021

Server-side invalid, expired, wrong-origin, reused and concurrent token tests already exist (`pairing-token.native.integration.test.ts`, `cloud/e2e/tests/pairing.spec.ts`). This adds the missing client tests in `usePairingState.test.tsx`:
- expired, invalid, instance-mismatch, rate-limited and generic failures show distinct guidance and never persist or install a credential
- blank input makes no request
- a double submit sends one request
- a pairing reply, or a credential save, that finishes after the server connection changed is dropped, with no stale token installed and a fresh code required for the new server

A superseded pairing now shows "server connection changed" instead of a generic failure. `useAppLifecycleEvents.test.tsx` pins that resume replay reloads only the conversation active when the debounced resume runs. The submit-contract scenarios from closed PR #29765 that still apply were ported (credited). Its production change is already superseded on develop.

47:T1c04,## #29919: 1:1 messaging egress through the identity that received the message

Refs #29919 (tracker; live provider certification remains open)

**Identity trace.** Telegram inbound to outbound is already bound on develop:
- The Worker edge attests `ELIZA_APP_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` through `getMe`, checking the bot id and username, before parsing the update, typing, replying or sending reminders (#29767).
- The gateway attests the same identity and hands off its connector account. The Worker rejects a mismatched account with 409 before provider egress.
- The D62 "chat not found" attribution was corrected on the tracker (D63/D64/D81). The live DM reached the correct bot and Worker, then ended `responded:false` with zero provider send.

This PR does three things:
- **Re-lands #30925** (credit @standujar). Private Telegram turns that complete with an empty or whitespace-only reply now send one stable notice through the exact-once ledger, in both the Worker edge and the gateway. The notice is classified `SharedRuntimeNoReplyError`, and the Shared route forwards `responded:false` / `no_response`. Media-only replies keep all distinct HTTPS links. Groups and membership events stay silent.
- **Blooio/iMessage fix (same bug class).** v4 webhooks carry the receiving line as `recipient`/`channel_address`, but only the v2 parser used it as the reply sender. v4 private replies therefore fell back to the configured project number, so a multi-number account could answer from a different line than the one the user wrote to. v4 now mirrors v2.
- **Telegram edge diagnostic.** When private egress is rejected with 400 "chat not found" or 403, the edge now logs a value-safe `recipientUnreachable` classification. That is the only runtime signal for a retired bot that shares the webhook secret, because a Telegram update does not name the bot that received it.

**Discord:** checked, no split. DMs are received and answered by the same gateway client, and proactive DMs use that same system bot.

**Consolidated leaves, checked against code:**
- **#29764:** failure classification, retry and fallback came in with #29766. The residual empty-reply silence is fixed here.
- **#29763:** identity attestation is present in the edge, gateway, reminder dispatch and release workflows (`verify-telegram-bot-identity.ts`).
- **#29206:** workflow resolver and preflight are present. Only the staging names-only and exact-SHA receipts remain.
- **#18235:** everything left is live: provider forward, agent ACK and sandbox attestation.

**Out of scope, still required:**
- Provider-backed live certification of first and repeat reply, reconnect and revocation for Telegram, Discord, Blooio and X.
- The staging Blooio isolation decision (#22787).
- Fixture custody.

**`AGENT_BACKUP_CATALOG_CYCLE_FAILED`:**
- **Where it fails.** Worker startup is config-only, and R2/Hetzner errors become reconciling states inside the cycle. The failure that escapes is therefore the first Cloud DB authority query.
- **Defect.** `deploy-eliza-provisioning-worker.yml` forwarded `DATABASE_SSL_NO_VERIFY` to the backup unit only when the catalog runtime gate was on. Staging runs deletion-authority-only, so the backup unit kept strict TLS against the same DB that the provisioning daemon reaches with the relaxed policy. Fixed.
- **Diagnostic.** Each cycle stage is now named, and the retry warning logs stage, error classes, allowlisted codes (SQLSTATE/TLS) and HTTP status. It never logs messages, hosts, keys or credentials, and the cycle still fails closed.
- **Still open.** The redeployed worker's log line will confirm the cause. Possible alternatives are DB reachability or role permissions.
- **Also noticed, unchanged.** This worker also runs the `database_erasure` phase for runnable deletion requests. That may be intended but should be confirmed.

Also still required: the signed-in same-ID Dedicated staging pass (adopt, chat, reload, suspend, resume).

48:T1602,Assistant-flow fixes across inbox, scheduling UI, notes, health, meeting-ghost, and the assistant prompt. There is one commit per issue.

## Fixes #25284: INBOX reply/approve now require turn-bound consent
- Before, the planner could send an INBOX reply by setting `confirmed: true`, and `approve` always sent.
- Now a send from the INBOX action goes through the same `requireSendConsent` gate as SEND_DRAFT. The consent is tied to the entry, the recipient message and the exact body text.
- Tapping the one-tap "Send" chip (value `inbox approve <id>`) counts as the owner's affirmative reply in a later turn. `requireSendConsent` gained an optional `affirmations` list to support this, and it is now exported from `@elizaos/plugin-assistant`.
- The `confirmed` parameter is removed from the planner's view. The owner HTTP routes keep their explicit UI decision: approve sends, and reply sends only when `confirmed` is set.
- I checked the other send actions: `scheduleDraftSend` and `respondToMessage` have no `confirmed` flag.
- Tests: `plugin-inbox` `test/inbox-action.test.ts` + `test/inbox-routes.test.ts` passed (44/44). They cover:
  - a fabricated `confirmed` or a same-turn approve does not send;
  - a later chip turn does send;
  - a refusal cancels the send.
- `plugin-assistant` `send-consent.persistence.test.ts` passed (1/1, extended with the chip-affirmation cases).

## Fixes #31891: "Run now" fires the scheduled item
- "Run now" on a manual item now calls the existing `POST /api/lifeops/scheduled-tasks/:id/fire` route through `client.fireScheduledTask`. The button is disabled while the request is in flight.
- Outcomes other than a successful fire (skipped, raced, deferred, failed) are shown as errors rather than reported as success.
- Acknowledge is still the verb for non-manual items.
- Tests: `packages/ui` `ScheduledTaskEditor.test.tsx` passed (8/8).

## Refs #31017: less duplicated context per assistant turn
This issue is only partly done here.

I added `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/scripts/measure-assistant-turn-context.ts`. It runs without a model, uses the core chars/3.5 estimator, and writes to `test-results/assistant-turn-context/summary.json`.

| Component (full PA tool surface) | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Planner `# Routing hints` block (duplicates the hint already on each native tool description) | 5,091 chars / ~1,455 tok | 0 |
| Stage 1 task text + TASK_AUTHORITY | 2,689 / ~769 | 2,558 / ~731 |
| lifeops provider standing instructions | 3,573 / ~1,021 | 3,340 / ~955 |
| **Total** | **11,353 / ~3,245** | **5,898 / ~1,686** |

- The routing-hint change is lossless:
  - a hint is left out only when its own wire tool's description already contains it;
  - hints no wire tool carries (promoted-family parents, the no-native-tools lane) are still rendered.
- No provider data, message bodies or tool results are dropped.
- Tests: new `routing-hints-dedupe.test.ts`, `compress-mode.test.ts` and `history-wire.test.ts` passed (14/14). Core `message-stage1-prompt-tier.test.ts` passed (31/31).

49:T14d9,A most-recent-note request currently sends all matching notes to the model to compare timestamps, then leaves UTC-to-local conversion to free-form prose. Select the requested recency basis in code and expose validated local display text beside the canonical timestamp.

`NOTES_LIST` accepts optional `latestBy=createdAt|updatedAt` after existing filters, returns every tie at the maximum instant, and retains complete selected records, source hashes and snapshot revision. Bare “latest” gains no hidden basis. Unselected LIST output remains unchanged.

Selected LIST and exact GET reads accept an explicit IANA `displayTimeZone`; otherwise validated current UI timezone metadata can supply the display zone. Selected LIST adds its selection display; an exact single-record GET adds both timestamp displays. Raw instants and note content remain unchanged. Invalid explicit zones/unsupported operations fail before writes; no valid display zone means no new label. These fields do not change extraction, model defaults, reply style, storage or permissions.

Six initial expectations exposed Intl comma-versus-“at” punctuation differences; a separate test-only commit accepts exactly those two separators while retaining anchored expected dates, times and zones. 4a:T2204,# Relates to

Completion evaluation currently shares the response-handler model slot, so changing its model also changes unrelated stages. This adds an optional evaluator-only selection without changing default stage models.

# Change

`ELIZA_EVALUATOR_MODEL` requests a provider model for completion evaluation only. An explicit nonblank evaluator argument wins; per-agent settings precede the documented environment fallback. Blank/nonstring runtime settings preserve the existing slot. Selection, including absence, remains fixed during restoration and is resolved afresh for a new invocation.

Both message-pipeline evaluator paths forward the host's bound settings/model metadata. Response handling, planning, extraction and background model selections remain unchanged. A per-call override does not borrow context/output limits belonging to another registered model; unknown capacity remains diagnostic and complete input is preserved. Actual served-model metadata remains authoritative if an adapter ignores the request or falls back.

Default off: no profile or model configuration is changed by this PR. The pinned develop base already includes the merged restoration/settled-scope work. This PR's diff is evaluator-only selection and its supporting budget, facade, documentation and tests, plus the mechanical Health CI dependency described below; it does not duplicate those merged fixes.

# Evidence Details

The six actual message-pipeline fixture cases cover ordinary and settled-action paths with runtime, environment and unset selection. They assert that only the evaluator receives the override, metadata getters retain their receiver, and the domain read executes once. Restoration tests preserve original-source recovery and model selection through setting changes; a fresh invocation observes the new setting. HTTP fixture tests inspect the actual SDK request model and reasoning fields, preserving the other stages' selections.

The legacy fixture envelopes remain valid against the now-merged restoration contracts. The refreshed 47 assistant tests cover those contracts without importing any additional restoration behavior in this PR. No external inference, deployed service, or whole-system quality improvement is claimed by these fixtures.

4b:T611,Native Cerebras Qwen coding trajectories lost private reasoning between tool calls. A fresh official run also showed that a progress reply consumed the planner's completion-verification allowance, and an indeterminate workspace observation produced the false fallback “I changed files.”

Preserve complete provider/model-bound reasoning in non-streaming native results, canonical model history, and recorded trajectories. Restore the supported Cerebras wire field with assistant identity checks, separately from visible replies and actual tool steps. Pending progress replies and empty responses receive bounded continuation without spending final-verification retries. Coding keeps native tool calls required, using REPLY to finish. Empty-response retries are consecutive and reset after successful tool progress. Final answers remain subject to verification, and uncertain workspace observations no longer produce claims of confirmed edits.

4c:T101c,Refs #31017. Planner and evaluator requests currently carry instructions and schema choices that their available tools and context cannot use. This change omits those inapplicable instructions while preserving complete conversation, provider, tool-result, and model content.

The planner scopes shell policy to the shell surface and coding delegation to `TASKS`/`TASKS_SPAWN_AGENT`. It retains the prohibition on delegating a single lookup, names only exposed `WEB_FETCH`/`WEB_SEARCH` alternatives, and applies the same mandatory rules to saved optimized prompts. Explicitly empty tool lists omit tool-specific rules; unspecified lists retain them.

Other changes in this PR:

- Advertise evaluator restoration routes only when deferred context can be restored, and omit receipt-selection fields when no committed receipt exists.
- State identical post-turn evidence contracts once and remove a reply directive from the RECENT_MESSAGES data provider.
- Offer canonical fact-memory fields in extraction schemas while retaining stored-alias readers; make DISCOVER_ACTIONS describe its actual catalog mode once.
- Exercise the real planner request boundary with 18 default/optimized-template regressions. Complete the calendar-history fixture's logger so the real executor's slow-operation logging does not fail the test.

4e:T56b,## Summary

Adds \plugin-automaton-token-safety\, an ElizaOS plugin providing real-time token safety, bytecode honeypot scanning, and risk analysis on Base L2:

- \AUTOMATON_SCAN\ — Real-time sub-200ms EVM bytecode scan for honeypots, mint backdoors, blacklists, and security risk score.
- \AUTOMATON_SENTINEL_LATEST\ — Continuous liquidity pool watcher capturing newly deployed pools and audited tokens on Base (Uniswap v3/v4 & Aerodrome).
- \AUTOMATON_APPROVAL_RISK\ — Audits ERC-20 allowances, drain risk, and unlimited approvals for tokens and spenders.
- \AUTOMATON_SIMULATE\ — Pre-flight dry-run swap execution measuring transfer taxes and execution traps.
- \AUTOMATON_LIQUIDITY_RISK\ — Deep DEX pool depth analysis, price impact (2% move USD), and reserve concentration.

All actions validate inputs with Zod schemas. Paid calls use x402 v2 challenge-response with caller-bound EIP-3009 TransferWithAuthorization in USDC on Base. Gasless buyer (zero ETH required).

## Configuration
- \AUTOMATON_API_BASE\: Default \https://api.automaton-sovereign.workers.dev\
- \AUTOMATON_SIGNER_KEY\: Optional key to sign EIP-3009 authorizations.
- \AUTOMATON_MAX_UNITS\: Per-call cap in USDC base units (default 10000 = .01).
- \AUTOMATON_DRY_RUN\: Set to 1 to sign without sending paid requests.

Official brand assets (400x400 logo.jpg, 1280x640 banner.jpg) included in \images/\.4f:T1b58,Three desktop/UI issues, one commit each. Each builds on the partial work merged in #32852 (#30744, #30676) and #29598 (#29597).

## #30744: packaged startup shows the ready UI before embedded first-run completes

Refs #30744 (code part; packaged retest still needed, see below)

#32852 already reclassified a restored loopback `remote` record on the shell's embedded API as `embedded-local`. It also made the backend's first-run state authoritative over a renderer-cached completion. This PR closes the two paths that could still reach `ready` against a backend whose runtime had not booted:

- **Runtime check before ready.** `starting-runtime` used to declare every `remote-backend` target ready without asking the backend. That included the shell's own embedded API reached through a restored record when the shell-mode RPC was unavailable, and a restored record right after onboarding, while the deferred runtime boot was still in flight. The result was desktop bootstrap `503 db_unavailable`.
  - It now reads the backend runtime state first.
  - A `running` agent keeps the immediate ready path.
  - `not_started`, `starting` or `error` goes through the agent-readiness loop. That loop starts the runtime, waits for `running`, and surfaces boot failures through the designed error state.
- **Shell-mode RPC reports the booted mode.** `desktopGetRuntimeMode` returned the env-only resolution. The main process actually boots with the deployment-aware, reachability-verified mode. The RPC now reports the booted mode, so the renderer classifies its restored target against whether the shell really hosts the embedded agent.

**Remaining:**
- Retest the packaged macOS build with 31337 occupied, and with returning configured profiles.
- The stuck "Opening Cloud sign-in" (Safari never opens) from the same report is not addressed. It needs a packaged repro.

## #30676: macOS chat microphone rejects after a Settings grant

Refs #30676 (code part; signed-build verification still needed)

Electrobun 1.18.1 has its own WKUIDelegate `requestMediaCapturePermissionForOrigin` handler (I read `nativeWrapper.mm` at v1.18.1). It shows an in-process NSAlert and caches Allow/Block per origin for the life of the process. Two consequences:
- A Block answered once stayed cached after the user granted access in Settings or System Settings.
- First-use Talk showed an app dialog instead of the macOS prompt.

`native/macos/window-effects.mm` now installs a media-capture policy on Electrobun's delegate class, which the main, detached Settings and surface windows all share:

- **Scope.** The policy applies to `views://` and to the renderer origin the host registers.
- **Decision.** Each request follows the live `AVCaptureDevice` authorization:
  - authorized: grant.
  - not determined: ask macOS (the same request Settings makes), and grant only if approved.
  - denied or restricted: deny.
- **No cache.** Decisions are not cached, so a later grant applies to the next capture.
- **Other origins.** Everything else keeps Electrobun's per-origin prompt, including browser-workspace pages on other loopback ports.

The host registers the renderer origin and installs the policy after creating the main window. It retries until the WKWebView delegate exists. The renderer-side native grant probe and the focus/permission-change re-probe from #32852 are unchanged.

**Remaining:** on a signed packaged build, verify real microphone transcription and reply, Settings grant carry-over and relaunch.

## #29597: shared UI composition and honest states

Refs #29597 (governance/inventory items dropped per triage)

Re-verified on develop:
- The Brand/LockOn/SurfaceBadge/CloudTextInput wrappers are gone (no references).
- Database, Memories, Tasks and Automations use `FRAMED_PAGE_LAYOUT` with `FramedPage gutterOwner="framed-page"`, so each has a single gutter, scroll and composer owner. Managed Cloud keeps its shell-owned scroll region.
- Relationships renders loading, error, empty and no-results through `PagePanel.ContentState`.
- I checked the linked child reports: Settings Account/Billing terminal states, App Permissions mutually exclusive branches, Notes Retry and no false zero count, and Calendar not stuck on loading are all fixed on develop.

Remaining honest-state defects fixed here:
- **Browser workspace.** A deterministic unavailability (missing route, or the typed non-retryable runtime boundary) no longer re-polls the known 404 every 2.5s. Transient failures keep polling.
- **Wallet.** A turned-off wallet showed "Your wallet is empty." although no balance was read. It now shows a distinct "Wallet is off." state above the Enable control. The empty-wallet flag is limited to a settled, enabled wallet with an account.

**Not done:** Browser New Tab still seeds the embeddable Google start page chosen in 73b7b03e1e3. #28865 asks for `about:blank`, which needs an owner decision.

54:T1232,## Change

The Chromium companion ships only with elizaOS images. Move its sources and Chromium patch tooling to `packages/os/browser`, remove its standalone workspace, and point the existing Linux/AOSP assembly and provenance checks at that owner. Standard OS build/typecheck/lint/test commands include the browser, and Turbo tracks its generated assets and environment. The wire codec is consumed through the locked `plugin-browser/native-wire` dependency. Installed Linux acceptance now requires the embedded component and installed native relay.

Remove the `remote-control-host` workspace by moving its controller and encrypted storage into `plugin-browser`. Update agent, app, cloud, UI and container consumers. Explicit server exports reject browser resolution; renderer clients keep their browser-safe leaf imports. This controller remains available to cloud/agent hosts rather than becoming an OS dependency.

Closes #32859. Uses **packages/os in this monorepo**; no separate OS repository transfer.

The exported remote browser plugin is a static declaration so the plugin-view inventory can validate it; the owner-bound factory preserves the same routes. A redundant, identical Capacitor mock property was removed from the Android login fixture to unblock UI lint/typecheck; all six fixture tests pass.

The relocated SQL test now separates disk-backed credential persistence from the 256-session capacity/recovery workload on real in-memory PGlite. All assertions remain; the existing timeout remains. Tests explicitly await SecretsService readiness instead of relying on slow disk operations to let it start. The remote-controller suite passes in 22.2 seconds. Its Bun command is also recognized by the repository native-test registration gate.

55:T13bb,Closes #31249

## What

- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/meeting-ghost/index.ts`: `MeetingGhostTranscript` gains an optional IANA `timeZone`. `parseDueDate` projects the meeting instant into that zone with `getZonedDateParts` and walks days with `addDaysToLocalDate` / `getWeekdayForLocalDate` (from `lifeops/time.ts`); the 17:00 ledger `dueAt` and the 09:00 calendar deadline are wall times in that zone built with `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`. An explicit `YYYY-MM-DD` is re-validated so an impossible day (e.g. `02-30`) yields no instant instead of rolling forward. The header documents the zone precedence: transcript `timeZone` -> owner's configured zone -> process zone.
- `consumer.ts`: `runMeetingGhostForTranscript` stamps a transcript that carries no zone with `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, <meeting instant>)` before analysis, so the reachable event path (`event-handler.ts`, whose finalized payload carries no zone) resolves on the owner's calendar day.
- Existing fixtures in `test/meeting-ghost.test.ts` and `test/meeting-ghost.integration.test.ts` are pinned to `timeZone: "UTC"` so their instant assertions stay deterministic on any host.
- New `test/meeting-ghost.local-due-dates.test.ts` pins the four cases from the issue (with the transcript zone, and again through the process-zone fallback), an explicit date, and an impossible date.

## Why

`parseDueDate` resolved "tomorrow"/weekday phrases on the UTC calendar day of `meetingStartedAt` (`setUTCDate`, `getUTCDay`, `toISOString().slice(0,10)`), and `dueDateToLedgerDueAt` / the calendar deadline pinned `T17:00:00Z` / `T09:00:00Z`. A Monday 5:30pm PT meeting is already Tuesday in UTC, so "by tomorrow" landed on Wednesday and "by Friday" a week out; a Monday 8am JST meeting is still Sunday in UTC, so "by tomorrow" landed on Monday itself and "by Monday" on the meeting day. The ledger `dueAt`, follow-up approvals, and "Deadline:" calendar intent all carried that day. Resolving on the meeting's local day and building the instants in that zone fixes every consumer at once.

56:Tff3,Closes #31248

## What

- `plugins/plugin-health/src/actions/health.ts` and `screen-time.ts`: the runners take a required `resolveTimeZone(runtime)` adapter and build the "today" key with the existing `getZonedDateParts`/`getLocalDateKey` helpers (`normalizeTimeZone` validates the returned zone). `context.date`, `data.date`, the HealthKit `--date` request, and the connector-summary pick all use that key.
- `HealthActionService.getHealthTrend(days, { timeZone })` carries the zone through LifeOps (`service-mixin-health.ts`, `service.ts`, `domains/health-service.ts`) to a new `HealthBridgeConfig.timeZone`; `getRecentSummaries` walks local days with `addDaysToLocalDate`, and the fixture backend's offset-zero day follows the same zone. `todayDateKeyUtc()` is gone.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/health.ts` and `screen-time.ts` supply `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, now)` (owner timezone fact, falling back to the process zone).
- Tests: `health.local-day.test.ts`, `screen-time.local-day.test.ts`, `health-bridge.local-day.test.ts` run the real runners / fixture bridge at `22:30Z` under `Asia/Tokyo`, `America/Los_Angeles`, `Pacific/Kiritimati`.

## Why

Every "today" in the health plugin came from `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, the UTC calendar day. HealthKit's `--date` is a device-local day and screen-time / connector rows are keyed by local day, so a Tokyo owner asking before 09:00 JST (or a Kiritimati owner before 14:00) was handed yesterday's day and the reply labelled it as today; the N-day trend ended on yesterday as well. The runners now resolve the owner's zone once per invocation and every day key and the trend walk are computed in that zone.

57:T31f5,# Relates to

Follow-through on #31379, which made the embedding service activate late on
`MODEL_REGISTERED`. Its shared-initialization design stays as is. The failure
half of the problem is already fixed on develop: `dispatcher.ts` now catches a
rejecting `MODEL_REGISTERED` observer and reports it as
`AgentRuntime.registerModel`. That makes the failure visible once. It does not
let the service recover. This PR is the recovery half.

# Change

On develop (`f6f7f1c15e`), when late activation fails (for example a
transient `runtime.createTask` failure while `TaskDrain` creates the
`EMBEDDING_DRAIN` task), the embedding service is dead until the process
restarts:

1. `initialize()` caches the rejected promise (`this.initialization ??=
   this.initializeQueue()`), so nothing ever creates the queue again.
2. `modelRegistrationHandler` has already set `isDisabled = false`, so every
   later `MODEL_REGISTERED` returns early at the `!this.isDisabled` guard and
   never retries.
3. The `EMBEDDING_GENERATION_REQUESTED` and `MESSAGE_SENT` handlers that
   `initializeQueue()` registered before failing stay attached. Every request
   awaits the cached rejection and rethrows it. `queueEmbeddingGeneration`
   reports each one as `AgentRuntime.embeddingGenerationRequest`, one report
   per memory, indefinitely.
4. `stop()` awaits the same cached rejection and rethrows it during teardown.

After this change a failed activation is withdrawn:

- `initialize()` clears the cached promise on rejection and unregisters the
  two handlers `initializeQueue()` added. `registerEvent` does not
  deduplicate, so without this a retry would attach them twice and enqueue
  every request twice.
- `modelRegistrationHandler` restores `isDisabled = true` and rethrows. The
  existing dispatch boundary reports the failure once, under its current
  scope; this PR adds no second report.
- `stop()` completes when it races an activation that fails (J6). The
  activation's own caller receives that failure.

Requests that were already awaiting the failed activation still reject, and
the emitter's existing J7 catch reports them. After the withdrawal no handler
is attached, which matches the designed "waiting for a model" state. Nothing
is dropped silently.

Scope: `packages/core/src/services/embedding.ts` plus one test file. No public
type, event or export changes.

- [x] Targets `develop`; rebased on `origin/develop` at `f6f7f1c15e`.

      invariant. No other docs describe this path.
