the real `AgentRuntime`, model registry, event bus, `BatchQueue`/`TaskDrain`,
and plugin-sql on in-memory PGlite. The only test double is a
`runtime.createTask` wrapper that injects the store failure.

- **Retry case.** The first `TEXT_EMBEDDING` registration fails on
  `createTask`. The test asserts: exactly one `AgentRuntime.registerModel`
  report; no drain task; no service handler left on
  `EMBEDDING_GENERATION_REQUESTED` or `MESSAGE_SENT`. A second registration
  must then create exactly one drain task and exactly one handler of each
  kind. A real request must drain and persist the vector to the database.
  There must be no further reports, no unhandled rejections, and a clean
  `stop()`.
- **Teardown race.** `stop()` runs while `createTask` is blocked, then
  `createTask` fails. `stop()` must resolve, the failure must be reported
  once, and no handlers may remain.

58:T12b7,Fixes #32412

## Problem

`plugins/plugin-native-inference/src/mobile-device-bridge-bootstrap.ts` built the bionic-host TEXT wire request as `maxTokens: lane.maxTokens ?? 256` (line 1834). When a chat turn set **no** output limit, the bridge still told the native host to stop at 256 tokens, and the post-generate check

```ts
if (typeof res.tokens === "number" && res.tokens >= baseRequest.maxTokens) { /* MODEL_OUTPUT_INCOMPLETE */ }
```

then rejected any reply with `tokens >= 256` as `MODEL_OUTPUT_INCOMPLETE` — even when the host had actually finished the answer. A warm installed-APK full-chat run reached this after ~115 s (`#32412`); the completed completion was silently dropped.

## Change

Mirrors the canonical contract already in `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/bionic-host-loader.ts`:

- **Omit `maxTokens` from the wire request when the caller supplied no limit** (the native host owns its own model capacity), instead of injecting a bridge-only `256`.
- Only apply the token-boundary rejection when a caller limit exists.
- Read the host's explicit `incomplete` receipt (added to `BionicGenerateResponse`) so a genuinely truncated native reply is still rejected when no caller limit was set.
- `outputReserveTokens` on `shared/native-model-request.ts` is now optional so the guard falls back to its own default reserve rather than a fabricated `256`.

Diff is bounded to the bionic-host branch of `makeGenerateHandler` plus the two small type widenings: `29 insertions, 7 deletions` across the two source files, plus one regression test.

## Reproduction (before → after)

A real abstract-namespace `AF_UNIX` host speaking the same 4-byte length-prefixed JSON frames as `ElizaBionicInferenceServer.java`, driven through the registered TEXT_LARGE handler.

Before the fix, a host reply of **4096 decoded tokens with no caller limit**:

```
ElizaError: Bionic local model output reached the decode boundary before a stop condition
context: { maxTokens: 256, outputTokens: 4096 }   // <- injected cap
```

After the fix the same reply resolves, the wire frame carries **no** `maxTokens`, an explicit caller limit is still forwarded and enforced, and a host `{ incomplete: true }` receipt still throws `MODEL_OUTPUT_INCOMPLETE`. Both the buffered and the streaming (`op:"generateStream"`) transports are covered.

```
plugins/plugin-native-inference/src/mobile-device-bridge-bootstrap.output-cap.test.ts
  ✓ omits maxTokens from the wire request when the caller sets no limit
  ✓ does not reject a host-completed long reply when no caller limit was set
  ✓ still rejects a native incomplete receipt with no caller limit
  ✓ forwards an explicit caller limit and rejects output that fills it
  ✓ serves a completion below an explicit caller limit
  ✓ keeps the uncapped contract on the streaming transport too
Tests  6 passed (6)
```

Ran on `upstream/develop` `eb157cac47`. The three other failures in the package suite (`*.bge`, `*-headless-boot`, `*-runtime-replacement`) are the pre-existing `Run SQLite storage with pinned Node 24.15.0 or Bun 1.4.2` host limitation, unrelated to this change. Biome `check` clean on the touched files.

> Note: a rebuilt installed artifact is required for full device qualification. These are transport-level regressions proving the cap is gone and the boundary/rejection contracts are preserved; they are not device proof.

5b:T11e7,## #32849: validateActionParams drops invalid optional parameters

Fixes #32849

When an optional parameter was supplied with a value that failed its schema, `validateActionParams` removed the value, or replaced it with `schema.default`, and still returned `valid: true` with no errors. It now reports that value the same way it reports required-parameter violations. Defaults apply only when the value is omitted. `integer` schemas now get an integrality check and the same `minimum`/`maximum` checks as `number` schemas.

Caller check: the planner executor (`execute-planned-tool-call.ts`) validates through `validateToolArgs`, which already reports optional violations. Planner behavior is unchanged. The only reference to `validateActionParams` in the repo is its public export.

## #30825: local backup size-limit failures

Refs #30825

Already on develop via #32836: `POST /api/backups` maps `AgentSnapshotBudgetExceededError` to a 413. The response carries `code`, `stage`, `unit`, `observed`, `limit` and `retryable: false`, plus an actionable message, with no paths or DB diagnostics. The route test is `packages/agent/test/backup-capture-http.test.ts`. The fields are `observed`/`limit` rather than `observedBytes`/`limitBytes` because the file-count stage reports files, not bytes, and `unit` says which.

This PR changes the Settings backup dialog. It shows the non-retryable refusal as-is. Any other create failure (generic 500, runtime not ready, transport) now says it may be temporary and can be retried.

**Local v2 streaming backups are not feasible in this issue:**
1. There is no local restore for capture-v2. The agent's restore routes use v1 snapshot restore only. The restore-v3 candidate staging was deliberately removed from `@elizaos/agent` (see `packages/agent/FOOTPRINT-REVIEW.md`), and the restore-v3 executor that remains lives only in Cloud's isolated staging. Writing v2 files that local restore cannot read would report backup success for artifacts that cannot be restored.
2. `createDefaultAgentBackupV2CaptureSources` rejects Postgres-backed agents (`AGENT_BACKUP_V2_POSTGRES_UNSUPPORTED`) and in-memory PGlite.
3. The PGlite database component still builds the whole tar.gz in memory. An available-memory gate (8× archive estimate) bounds it, so it is not a constant-memory stream either.

Local large-state backup needs its own work item: a local v2 restore that swaps in the PGlite data dir, media, state files and vault, plus list/prune support for the new format. After that, a real round-trip above 128 MiB must be verified.

- `vitest run src/components/settings/AdvancedSection.test.tsx` (packages/ui): 7 passed, including 2 new tests (size-limit refusal shown as-is; unexpected failure marked retryable)
- `bun run typecheck` (packages/ui): clean
- `npx vitest run test/backup-capture-http.test.ts` (packages/agent): 3 passed. An earlier run under heavy machine load had one failure; the rerun passed.

5c:T2f9b,# Relates to

Kept as `Relates to` rather than `Closes` so #30904 stays open to track the pre-existing passthrough/prose-mode split: `drainPassthrough` (`streaming.ts:751`) emits its buffer verbatim, so a literal surrogate pair split across `push()` boundaries in passthrough mode is still delivered malformed. That path is explicitly scoped out here (see Risks) and is the same holdback shape applied to a different drain; flagged by @hermesagent270-commits' executed review at `ef9ca36` and left as a follow-up.

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to `ResponseSkeletonStreamExtractor.appendVisibleAndEmit` (chunk-boundary logic) plus a new end-of-stream `flushPendingSurrogate` step in `flush()`. Field routing, ordering, span parsing, reasoning-tag filtering, and passthrough mode are untouched. For any content with no lone surrogate at a chunk boundary the behavior is byte-identical (the ASCII/BMP control test proves this). The only observable change is that an emitted chunk is now always well-formed UTF-16: a trailing lone high surrogate is held back for its low half, and any other unpaired surrogate is replaced with U+FFFD.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`ResponseSkeletonStreamExtractor` (`packages/core/src/utils/streaming.ts`) is the per-token stream handed to clients (constructed in the model dispatcher, feeding `downstreamChunk` → `paramsChunk`/`ctxChunk`). It could deliver a chunk that is not well-formed UTF-16: a non-BMP code point (e.g. an emoji 😀) was split into its two UTF-16 surrogate code units emitted as two separate `onChunk` chunks. Two independent paths caused it:

1. **Escaped path** — when a provider serializes a non-BMP char as a JSON escape pair (`\uXXXX\uXXXX`), `processActiveString` decoded each `\uXXXX` escape independently and called `appendAndEmit` immediately, so the two halves emitted as separate chunks inside a single `push()`.
2. **Literal path** — the loop consumes one UTF-16 code unit at a time, so an unescaped emoji split whenever a `push()` boundary fell between its halves.

Clients encode each individual chunk into UTF-8 SSE frames / WebSocket text frames / `JSON.stringify`, all of which corrupt a lone surrogate.

**Fix:** both paths funnel through `appendVisibleAndEmit`, so the surrogate handling lives there once. When the chunk about to be emitted ends on a lone high surrogate, that code unit is held back and prepended to the next emission once its trailing low surrogate arrives. Any *interior* unpaired surrogate that remains in the releasable prefix — an interior lone high not followed by a low (`A\ud83dB`) or an isolated low (`A\ude00B`) — is replaced with U+FFFD via `toWellFormedUnicode` before emission; the replacement is 1:1 by code unit, so the length-based emission cursor stays valid. `emittedContent` therefore stays well-formed and is the running join of emitted chunks (also passed as the `accumulated` argument, which stays consistent and well-formed), and `fieldContents` mirrors the well-formed emitted value plus at most one still-raw pending high surrogate. At genuine end-of-stream, `flushPendingSurrogate` emits any still-held lone high surrogate as U+FFFD — matching the codebase's existing well-formed convention — rather than dropping a real character or shipping a lone surrogate.

This second sanitization step closes the P2 review finding ([`ss251`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/30919#pullrequestreview)): withholding only a *trailing* high surrogate left non-trailing unpaired surrogates reaching `onChunk` unchanged.

No character is lost or duplicated: joining all emitted chunks for a field reconstructs the original decoded string; only chunk boundaries within a field change so they never fall between surrogate halves.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. Behavior of a public streaming utility is made correct (well-formed chunks) without changing its API.

# Testing

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
cd packages/core
bun run test src/utils/__tests__/streaming-field-events.test.ts
```

Covered: escaped emoji `\ud83d\ude00` in a single `push()`; literal emoji split across two `push()` boundaries; a genuinely lone trailing high surrogate at `flush()` (→ U+FFFD); and an ASCII/BMP control that stays byte-identical and still streams incrementally. Each test asserts `c.isWellFormed()` for every emitted chunk and that `chunks.join("")` reconstructs the original string.

5d:T689,Fixes #28646

## Problem

Legacy email-only accounts can disagree on phone verification: `users.phone_verified=false` while `user_identities.phone_verified=NULL` when both phone numbers are absent. Migration 0051 projected NULL in that case. `projectionMatchesUser()` required strict equality, so otherwise coherent phone/Telegram convergence was rejected as `identity_projection_conflict`.

Phone routing was already fail-closed (same number and TRUE on both rows). Fresh signup copies `false`. Returning login exited early when Steward authority already matched, so it never normalized the legacy field.

## Approach

- Distinguish matching Steward authority (same user + Steward subject) from full projection parity.
- Treat the phoneless `false`/`NULL` tuple as projection-coherent drift.
- Append-only repair: write `phone_verified=false` only for that tuple, only on the caller's own identity row.
- Healthy returning login stays a no-write fast path.
- Enforce `phone_verified=true` ⇒ `phone_number` is non-null; new rows keep default-false semantics via `COALESCE` and schema default.
- Never claim or overwrite an identity owned by another user.

## Tests

Deterministic repository/service tests only (isolated PGlite + unit). No live DB, staging, or real-user mutation.

- Legacy false/NULL repair
- Healthy idempotence (no `updated_at` write)
- Phone/Telegram convergence eligibility
- Phone routing non-regression (still requires TRUE on both rows)
- Ownership skip when Steward subjects do not match
- CHECK: TRUE without a phone number is rejected

Unpatched `develop`: convergence inspect returned `identity_projection_conflict`. After the fix: `eligible`.5e:T2fbd,Notes read tools inherited a long family-wide routing recipe, including PATCH advice that contradicted its required snapshot revision. Keep only shared Notes routing rules in that hint; operation details remain in existing tool schemas. Clarify that every PATCH requires a revision, while UPDATE supports revision-free literal substitution.

The shared hint is 791 characters shorter. PATCH/UPDATE parameter descriptions each add 33 characters for the corrected rule. Tool schema shapes, handlers and permission gates are unchanged. Two synthetic smoke-note bodies also regain their schema-2 separators so fixtures reconstruct authored content correctly; no stored user data or renderer code changes.

This is the Notes-only recut of the closed broader contribution, based on current develop. It does not claim to fix the separate Home classification or Calendar date-model errors.

Validation on exact head **0283cc70b50cb722138fb85cd9bf5eb3f34c83a5** after develop sync: 253 Notes tests; Notes typecheck and lint passed. See the verification summary below. No fresh isolated-head live inference or renderer run is claimed.

Earlier integrated live testing returned the correct note/event with unchanged notes: four calls, 56,640 input tokens including 3,072 cached, and 6.834 seconds. That was slower than the earlier sample; it is not a causal speedup or a fresh live run of this isolated branch. Prior fixture visual evidence remains tied to its original revision; no new renderer smoke run is claimed here. Full private captures and the input explorer remain local.

# Risks

Medium-low, Fitbit only. `syncFitbit` reads `profile.user.timezone` (validated through `Intl`), converts every wall time with the existing DST-correct `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`, falls back to `offsetFromUTCMillis` with a warning, and throws `HealthConnectorApiError` (recorded as `lastSyncError` by the personal-assistant sync boundary) when the profile has neither; the episode persists the zone and the weight sample's `localDate` is the log's own date. Strava, Withings and Oura are untouched. The recorded Fitbit contract test's pinned instants move from the host zone to the Europe/London profile zone of its fixture.

Makes Fitbit instants host-independent so sleep-regularity and circadian rules see the user's real bedtime and wake time.

Bug fix (non-breaking).

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

61:T1bf1,# Relates to

Fixes #32511 — "Android restaging overwrites the real musl loader when wrapper exceeds size heuristic".

# Change

`stageSeccompShimForAbi` (`packages/app/scripts/lib/stage-android-agent.ts`) decides whether the staged `<ldName>` is the Alpine musl loader (which must be relocated to `<ldName>.real`) or our own `loader-wrap` executable (already in place from a prior run). It used a size heuristic: `size >= 200 * 1024 ⇒ loader`.

That heuristic is unsound because it compares against a constant instead of the file's identity. On arm64 the compiled static wrapper is ~1 MiB (1,064,168 bytes reported in #32511), i.e. larger than the cutoff, so a **second** staging pass classifies the wrapper as the loader and:

1. `copyFileSync(stagedLoader, stagedRealLoader)` — overwrites the real musl loader with the wrapper,
2. `rmSync(stagedLoader)` — removes the wrapper,
3. re-stages the wrapper as `<ldName>`.

The net result is `<ldName>` and `<ldName>.real` both containing the wrapper, so the runtime `execve` chain (wrapper → `.real`) can never reach musl and the on-device agent silently never comes up. This is silent data loss of the real loader, not a cosmetic defect.

The fix identifies the loader by explicit ELF identity instead of size:

- helper `readElfType()` parses the ELF header `e_type` (shared object = `ET_DYN`);
- the staged file is treated as the Alpine loader only when it is an ELF shared object **and** is not byte-identical to our cached wrapper.

Any other content (our `ET_EXEC` wrapper, or an unknown/partial file) is left alone, which is the safe outcome: the good `.real` loader is preserved, and the wrapper is re-staged over `<ldName>`.

## Risk

Same-ABI/idempotent behavior is unchanged for the first pass (a genuine Alpine loader is an `ET_DYN` shared object and is relocated as before). The only behavioral change is the second-pass classification, which is the defect. The x86_64 path, which never tripped the heuristic, behaves identically.

# Testing

Executed the real exported function twice against a populated cache/assets layout (wrapper 1,064,168 B `ET_EXEC`, Alpine loader 600 KiB `ET_DYN`). Before the fix the second pass clobbers `.real`; after the fix it is preserved.

Regression test added: `packages/app/scripts/stage-android-agent.test.ts` — "SIGSYS shim restaging preserves the real loader when the wrapper exceeds 200 KiB (#32511)".

```

ok 3 - SIGSYS shim restaging preserves the real loader when the wrapper exceeds 200 KiB (#32511)
# tests 3
# pass 3
# fail 0
```

The new test **fails** on the pre-fix source and **passes** with the fix (verified via `git stash` of the lib change):


62:T1a90,# Relates to

Closes #31004 — original scope: shared-reference handling across diagnostic serializers and redactors.

# Change

`stringifyForDiagnostics` (`packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts`),
`serializeTtsDebugDetail` (`packages/ui/src/utils/tts-debug.ts`), and
`redactValue` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/redact-sensitive-data.ts`)
each tracked **every object ever visited** in a single, never-pruned set. So any
value reachable from two places (a DAG — the same object/array referenced from
two keys) was serialized with the *second* reference replaced by `"[Circular]"`,
silently dropping data from recorded trajectories, evaluator diagnostics, TTS
debug lines, and redacted audit payloads. True cycles *were* collapsed and the
first reference rendered correctly, which is why this survived the first-byte
and integrity checks.

Fix: track only the **current path's ancestors**, not all visited nodes.

- The two `JSON.stringify` replacers prune an ancestor stack back to the visited
  key's holder (`this`) and test membership against that stack, mirroring the
  already-corrected pattern in
  `packages/ui/src/components/developer/trajectory-reader-data.ts:95-117`.
- `redactValue` now removes each node from `seen` after its subtree is walked
  (and returns the mapped array before deleting).

Material risk: none identified. The output for a *true* cycle is unchanged
(`"[Circular]"`), and a DAG now serializes to exactly what `JSON.stringify`
produces natively for a shared non-cyclic reference.

# Testing

Verified by importing the **real modified source modules** (Node type-stripping,
Node v22.23.1, no build step, no mocks) and exercising them directly.

64:T889,Partial fix for #31017. A gateway response containing only `prompt_tokens`, only `completion_tokens`, or only `total_tokens` currently fabricates missing counts as zero. That makes incomplete telemetry look suitable for token-based cost estimation.

Normalize usage only when both input and output counts are present. Native buffered, SSE, and non-SSE fallback results preserve incomplete usage as unavailable and retain the original provider metadata. Responses uses the same normalization and derives a missing total from known input/output counts. Explicit zero counts remain valid. Model content and server-side credit debits are unchanged.

This addresses the remaining unknown-usage case after #27744. It does not complete #31017's context optimization or live trajectory comparisons.

65:T8a3,When a configured remote backend's startup status probe fails, the startup flow skipped the readiness wait and announced `AGENT_RUNNING`. This follow-up to #32862 treats an unavailable status as unproven readiness: it uses the existing bounded polling path, recovers when the backend starts, and presents the existing timeout state if it stays unavailable.

**Status probe classification** (`readRemoteRuntimeState` now uses the existing `runStartupProbe`):
- `running`: immediate-ready path (unchanged).
- HTTP 404, meaning the backend does not expose `/api/status`: keeps the previous immediate-ready path. This addresses @mtcow's review point that self-hosted backends without the endpoint must not regress into `agent-timeout`.
- Transport or other failure, or a response with no `state`: enters the bounded readiness loop.
- Any other reported state: enters the readiness loop (unchanged).

- The retry test now asserts that `getStatus` is called twice.

66:Td4a,# Relates to

Fixes #31891

## Summary

The scheduled-item editor labels its primary control **Run now** for manual starters but calls the `acknowledge` lifecycle verb. Acknowledging only changes state — the canonical runner never dispatches on acknowledge — so the owner presses "Run now" and **no run happens**. The UI test asserted the same wrong verb, so it kept passing while the feature was broken.

- `ScheduledTaskEditor` manual-starter primary button now calls `client.fireScheduledTask(taskId)` — the existing authenticated fire API (`POST /api/lifeops/scheduled-tasks/:id/fire`) that runs the task immediately through the same strict-fire path the scheduler tick uses. No new server surface; the API was already there, unused by this panel.
- Typed fire outcomes render visibly instead of being swallowed: `fired` succeeds; `raced` (another tick claimed the row — the run is happening) is treated as success without a misleading error; `skipped` / `dispatch_deferred` / `dispatch_failed` surface their `reason`/`error` in the panel's existing error line.
- **Acknowledging a delivered item remains its own operation**: non-manual tasks keep the `Acknowledge` label and still route `apply("acknowledge")`. The button's `onClick` branches on `isManual`, so both behaviors coexist.
- Test updates: the "Run now" test now asserts the **fire** call and explicitly asserts `applyScheduledTask` was NOT called (the old test pinned the bug); two new failure-sensitive tests cover visible `dispatch_failed` reporting (no `onApplied`, error text rendered) and `raced`-as-success; the verb-error test rejects `fireScheduledTask` since that is now the call "Run now" makes. `snooze`/`complete`/`dismiss` routing untouched.

## Definition of Done

- [x] Targets `develop`, zero conflicts.
- [x] Root cause verified in source before editing: `ScheduledTaskEditor.tsx` line 140 wired `Run now` → `apply("acknowledge")`; `runner.ts` confirms acknowledge is a non-terminal state change with no dispatch; `client.fireScheduledTask` and its route (`scheduled-tasks.ts` `matchTaskFire` + typed `serializeFireResult`) already existed.
- [x] Sabotage check: reverting the editor hunk makes the updated "Run now" test fail on `fireScheduledTask` not being called — the test discriminates the defect.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"nvidia","model":"z-ai/glm-5.3","client":"Hermes Agent","skill_revision":"local:slop-eliza/skills/slop-cash-contribute"} -->67:T1694,Follow-up to #30264 (merged as `bc84c05273`). I reviewed that PR at head `8ceb43c0a2` and raised this as a non-blocking note; it merged without it. The blocking finding from that review — an `./engine` mock leaking from `birdeye-handler.error-policy.test.ts` into `birdeye-handler.timeout.test.ts` — **is** resolved: the merged version rewrote that suite onto reserve/reconcile, and the whole proxy directory now runs clean in one shared process (`102 pass / 0 fail across 19 files`). This PR is only the remaining half.

## The defect

`resolveInferenceCredentialAdmissionDenial` (`generative-route-auth.ts:150`) unwraps `SuppressedError` by inspecting `.error` alone:

```ts
error.name === "SuppressedError" &&
"error" in error &&
error.error instanceof InferenceCredentialRevokedError
```

`SuppressedError.error` is the **newer** failure — the one disposal raised — and `.suppressed` is the one it displaced. So that condition matches only one of the two orderings this code can produce:

| ordering | `.error` | `.suppressed` | handled |
|---|---|---|---|
| body succeeds, disposal rejects with the revocation | revocation | route failure | ✅ today |
| **body fails with the revocation, disposal then throws its own error** | **disposal failure** | **revocation** | ❌ dropped |

## It is reachable with only existing code

`deferredCredentialAdmissionGuard`'s disposal throws when a credential is present but no organization resolved:

```ts
if (!organizationId) {
  throw new Error("Deferred inference credential is missing its organization");
}
```

Drive that under `await using` while the body raises the revocation and you get the second row. Executed on `develop`:

```
name         SuppressedError
.error       Error                            ← "missing its organization"
.suppressed  InferenceCredentialRevokedError
denialStatus null
mappedStatus null
```

`resolveInferenceCredentialAdmissionDenial` returns `null`, `asGenerativeCacheApiError` returns `null` with it, and **the route answers 500 instead of 401** — a revoked credential is reported to the caller as a server fault. The forward ordering, on the same head, correctly maps to 401.

## The change

Take the revocation from whichever slot holds it, and log the *other* one as the displaced failure so the existing log line keeps its meaning in both orderings:

```ts
const raised = "error" in error ? error.error : undefined;
const displaced = "suppressed" in error ? error.suppressed : undefined;
const revocation =
  raised instanceof InferenceCredentialRevokedError ? raised
  : displaced instanceof InferenceCredentialRevokedError ? displaced
  : undefined;
...
const companion = revocation === raised ? displaced : raised;
```

The `ApiError` shaping of the logged companion and the redaction behaviour are unchanged — the existing test that asserts no secrets reach the log still passes untouched.

## Verification

The regression test builds the ordering through a real `await using` rather than constructing a `SuppressedError` directly, because the `SuppressedError` global is not defined in this package's vitest environment — a direct construction throws `ReferenceError` and would have looked like a passing test only if written carelessly.

| | result |
|---|---|
| `generative-route-auth.test.ts` at head | **68 passed** (67 existing + 1 new) |
| restrict the search back to `.error` | **1 failed / 67 passed** |
| log the wrong companion (`companion = displaced`) | **1 failed / 67 passed** |
| `guarded-paid-proxy.test.ts`, `paid-route-standing.test.ts` (bun) | 4 pass / 0 fail, 8 pass / 0 fail |
| `biome check` on both files | clean |
| `tsc --noEmit` `packages/cloud/api` | exit 0 |

68:Tc47,Closes #31239

## What

`PUT /api/permissions/trade-mode` in `packages/agent/src/api/permissions-routes-extra.ts` now captures the previous `features` object and `tradePermissionMode` before writing. If `ctx.saveElizaConfig` throws, the handler restores exactly the prior in-memory state (removes a `features` object it created, removes a key that did not exist, or puts the old value back), logs at error level, and answers a structured 500 `Failed to save trade permission mode: <cause>` (error-policy J1) instead of `ok: true`. Modeled on the `POST /api/connectors` rollback in `connector-routes.ts`.

Tests: `permissions-routes-extra.mode-persistence.test.ts` gains two failing-write cases (an existing `agent-auto` mode stays `agent-auto` with sibling keys intact; a first-ever write leaves `features` undefined so the resolver falls back to `user-sign-only`). The dispatcher suite's "returns the updated mode when persistence throws" test pinned the old fabricated success and is rewritten to assert the 500, the rollback, and that `json` is never called.

## Why

The handler mutated `state.config.features.tradePermissionMode` in memory, wrapped the disk write in a catch that only `logger.warn`ed, and unconditionally answered 200 with the new mode. The in-memory value lived until restart, when the on-disk value returned, so an owner who switched **off** `agent-auto` trading saw success and the agent was auto-trading again after the next restart with no indication anything failed. Repository policy is that a failed write must not be reported as success and the in-memory state must not diverge from what was persisted.

## Evidence

```
     × removes a features object it created when the first-ever trade-mode write fails 2ms
 FAIL  ... > rolls the trade mode back and answers 500 when the config write fails

      Tests  2 failed | 11 skipped (13)
```

Green after the fix (`bunx vitest run src/api/permissions-routes-extra.mode-persistence.test.ts src/api/permissions-routes-extra.test.ts`):

69:Te06,Closes #31238

## What

`packages/agent/src/api/wallet-evm-balance.ts` gains one helper, `requireJsonRpcResult`, that rejects any keyed-provider body whose parsed envelope carries an `error` member (JSON-RPC object, or the bare string Alchemy's NFT REST API can emit). It throws a typed `ElizaError` with code `EVM_PROVIDER_RPC_ERROR`, context `{ provider, chain, chainId, rpcCode }`, and the provider's own message. The helper wraps the Alchemy `eth_getBalance` and `alchemy_getTokenBalances` calls, the Ankr `ankr_getAccountBalance` call, and both NFT calls (`getNFTsForOwner`, `ankr_getNFTsByOwner`). The public-RPC path keeps its existing `data.error?.message` check unchanged.

Five new tests in `wallet-evm-balance.test.ts` pin the 200-with-error envelope on each path: balances assert `error !== null` carrying the provider message with no zero balances presented as healthy; the NFT paths assert the envelope is rejected and logged with the chain and provider message rather than silently read as an empty collection (`WalletEvmNftCollection` has no error field, so the existing per-chain NFT boundary is kept as-is; widening that public DTO is out of scope here).

## Why

Alchemy and Ankr answer HTTP 200 with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"error":{...}}` for compute-unit throttling, invalid keys, and unsupported chains. `jsonOrThrow` only rejected non-2xx or non-JSON bodies, and the keyed paths read a missing `result` as `0n` / `[]`, so every throttled chain was emitted as `nativeBalance: "0", tokens: [], error: null` and `makeEvmChainFailure` never fired. The wallet UI then showed $0 on every EVM chain, and `wallet-balance-delta` (which excludes only chains with `error !== null`) took the false zero as a real sample and produced spurious "dropped to zero" / "restored" notifications. With the helper the throw reaches the existing per-chain `catch` in `fetchEvmBalances`, which reports it through `makeEvmChainFailure` with the provider text.

## Evidence

Red on develop `5b183d21ff25` (`bunx vitest run src/api/wallet-evm-balance.test.ts -t "JSON-RPC error envelopes"` in `packages/agent`):

```
     × reports an Alchemy token-balance error envelope even when the native call succeeded 6ms
     × reports an Ankr account-balance error envelope as a chain failure 6ms
     × rejects an Alchemy NFT error envelope instead of reading it as an empty collection 4ms
     × rejects an Ankr NFT error envelope instead of reading it as an empty collection 1ms
 FAIL  ... > reports an Alchemy native-balance error envelope as a chain failure
AssertionError: expected null not to be null
      Tests  5 failed | 32 skipped (37)
```

Green after the fix (`bunx vitest run src/api/wallet-evm-balance.test.ts src/api/wallet-evm-balance.surrogate.test.ts`):

# Background

Hardening of the outbound address classifier found during a 2026-09-19 review pass of the SSRF guards (reported privately through the repository's security advisory flow; no public issue).

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased/merged onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts (`1eba34ba95`).
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync: 373/373 workspace tasks and all repository audits, exit 0 on `ea0086b083` with the pinned Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0 install.

# Risks

Low. One additional check, `isNat64LocalUsePrefix`, in `isPrivateIpv6` in `packages/core/src/network/ssrf.ts` and its parity copy `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/security/outbound-url.ts`: every address whose first three hextets are `64:ff9b:1` (the RFC 8215 local-use block `64:ff9b:1::/48`) is classified as internal before any embedded-IPv4 decoding runs. The block is reserved for site-local translators, is not globally reachable, and a translator may carve its network-specific prefix at any length from /48 to /96, so decoding one layout would still miss the others; failing closed on the whole /48 is the only classification that covers them all. The well-known prefix `64:ff9b::/96` keeps its existing embedding-based rule (a public embedding stays public), and every other IPv6 range is untouched. One existing test asserted `64:ff9b:1::a9fe:a9fe` was public "because it is not /96"; that case now uses `64:ff9b:2::a9fe:a9fe`, which is outside both NAT64 prefixes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

RFC 8215 reserves `64:ff9b:1::/48` for local-use NAT64 translators. A network running such a translator could previously reach an embedded loopback, link-local or RFC 1918 target through a literal IPv6 URL that both guards classified as public; the DNS-pinned path still screened resolved addresses, so the literal form was the residual. The first commit unwrapped the low 32 bits of `64:ff9b:1::/96` like the well-known prefix; review pointed out that RFC 6052 allows the IPv4 address to sit at other offsets inside a /48 or /64 network-specific prefix, which that decode missed. The second commit replaces the decode with a fail-closed rule on the whole /48 in both guards.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (security hardening, non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

Before the change, `isPrivateIpAddress` returned `false` for `64:ff9b:1::7f00:1` and `64:ff9b:1::a9fe:a9fe` while returning `true` for the `64:ff9b::` spellings of the same addresses. At this head (`ea0086b083`): core network suites 49/49 with seven blocked local-use rows (`/96` embeddings, a site-subnet spelling `64:ff9b:1:abcd::a9fe:a9fe`, the `/64` translator layouts `64:ff9b:1:0:a:0:100:0` and `64:ff9b:1:0:7f:0:100:0`, the `/48` layout `64:ff9b:1:a00:1::`, and `64:ff9b:1::808:808`, which is internal because the local-use block is never globally routable even when it embeds a public address); cloud outbound-url 77/77 with six blocked local-use rows. `64:ff9b::808:808` and `64:ff9b:2::7f00:1` remain public. Biome clean on the four files.

6d:T1e5d,## The defect

`stripModelWrappers` in `packages/core/src/actions/json-model-output.ts` recognised a private-reasoning preamble like this:

```ts
const thinkEnd = candidate.indexOf("</think>");
if (candidate.startsWith("<think>") && thinkEnd !== -1) { … }
```

An exact lowercase `<think>` at position 0. But the codebase already has a canonical vocabulary for this in `packages/core/src/utils/reasoning-tags.ts`:

```ts
export const REASONING_TAG_NAMES = [
  "think", "thinking", "analysis", "reasoning", "reflection", "thought", "antthinking",
] as const;
```

matched case-insensitively, tolerant of whitespace inside the tag (`< thinking >`), with a documented repair for a **close-only residue** left by an earlier stripping pass — the evaluator's `None</think>`, referenced from `planner-loop.ts` and `evaluator.ts` as #20080.

The parser knew none of that. Measured against 20 realistic inputs it handled **12**. The 8 it missed:

| input | result on `develop` |
|---|---|
| `None</think>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `<thinking>r</thinking>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `<reasoning>r</reasoning>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `<analysis>r</analysis>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `<reflection>r</reflection>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `<THINK>r</THINK>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `< think >r</ think >{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |
| `Sure!\n<think>r</think>{"a":1}` | preamble reaches `JSON.parse` |

In every one `parseJsonModelOutput` returns `null` and the entire model output is discarded as unparseable. `parseJsonModelRecord` / `parseJsonModelArray` inherit it, and so do the callers that funnel through them (`plugin-inbox`'s triage classifier and priority scorer, `plugin-calendar`'s detail parser).

## Why the obvious fix is wrong

The tempting change is to call `stripReasoningBlocks`. **Don't** — and this is the part that makes the narrow original defensible. Reasoning markup is also ordinary string data. Run real payloads through that helper:

| payload | `stripReasoningBlocks` |
|---|---|
| `{"text":"<think>hi</think>"}` | `{"text":""}` — **value silently rewritten** |
| `{"a":1,"note":"</think> x"}` | **parse fails** |
| `{"prompt":"use <thinking> tags"}` | **parse fails** |

Corrupting a value is strictly worse than leaving a preamble in place, and `stripReasoningBlocks` is right for its own job — user-visible egress text, where the goal is to remove markup wherever it appears. A JSON parser has the opposite constraint.

## The fix

Both branches stay **anchored to the head of the candidate**, so nothing inside a payload can be touched:

1. **A reasoning tag opens the candidate.** Then nothing up to its close can be payload — slice through the first close after it. First-close semantics, exactly matching the previous `indexOf` behaviour.
2. **Close-only residue.** Strip through the first close tag only when no payload opener — `` ` ``, `{`, `[` — precedes it. A close tag inside a JSON string or a fenced body therefore always has an opener before it and is never touched.

Matching goes through `findNextOpenTag` / `findNextCloseTag`, so it inherits the canonical tag vocabulary and the deliberately non-quadratic scan those functions document.

An unmatched open tag is still preserved so the parse fails closed — same rule `reasoning-tags.ts` gives for `stripReasoningPrefixes`, and for the same reason: deleting only the open tag would turn a private payload into apparently-clean output.

## Results

**12/20 → 20/20**, no regression on any of the 12 that already passed:

| group | cases | before | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| preambles that should be stripped | 12 | 4 | 12 |
| payloads that must be left alone | 5 | 5 | 5 |
| fenced / array / fail-closed | 3 | 3 | 3 |

The five payload-safety cases are the ones the anchoring exists for, and they pass identically before and after.

**Liveness control.** Reverting only the source file fails exactly 8 of the 19 tests — the same 8 the baseline measurement predicted.

**Performance.** `reasoning-tags.ts` records that a maintainer benchmarked a regex form of this scan going quadratic on malformed residue, so I checked the property survives: 16,000 malformed `<think ` candidates (112,007 chars) parse in under 0.1 ms; a 500,000-char close-only prefix in 0.5 ms.

| check | result |
|---|---|
| `packages/core` `src/actions` + `src/utils` + `src/services/message` | 1817 passed / 1818 |
| `tsc --noEmit` `packages/core` | 0 errors |
| `plugins/plugin-inbox` (full) | 238 passed, 2 skipped |
| `plugins/plugin-calendar` (full) | 713 passed, 4 skipped |
| `biome check` | clean |

The single failure is `src/utils/project-registry.test.ts`, the machine-state fixture defect fixed by #30537 and not on this branch.

## Not done here — five sibling copies

The same `startsWith("<think>")` + `indexOf("</think>")` idiom appears in five more places:

- `packages/cloud/api/src/stubs/elizaos-core.ts:1048`
- `packages/ui/stories/src/eliza-core-browser-shim.ts:155`
- `plugins/plugin-calendar/src/internal/detail.ts:93`
- `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/triage-classifier.ts:253`
- `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/priority-scoring.ts:188`

I fixed only core's, deliberately. The three plugin copies run their local strip and then call `parseJsonModelRecord`, which runs core's — so for the JSON path this one change repairs them downstream, and it is the high-leverage edit. The other two are deliberate mirrors of core; I looked for a drift-check test enforcing that mirroring and found none, which is itself worth a maintainer's attention. Happy to follow up on any subset if you'd like them converged.

# Risks

Low. A single asynchronous accounting readback is removed from one lane
(`collectAffiliateInferenceFallback`), replaced with the value the debit
transaction already committed and returned. No schema, funding-ownership, or
flag changes. The synchronous pre-provider SQL policy checks and all other
gateway overhead are untouched.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The fenced affiliate inference settlement in `collectAffiliateInferenceFallback`
(`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/credits.ts`) issued a **second**
`organizations` balance query (`getOrganizationBalanceSnapshot`) **after** its
atomic debit transaction had already returned the authoritative post-debit
balance and revision. Ordinary deferred/direct settlement already publishes its
committed debit result directly via `republishOrgBalanceHintAfterDebit` without
a readback.

This PR makes the affiliate lane behave identically: it publishes the
transaction's own `outcome.newBalance` / `outcome.balanceRevision` to the
admission fence (`publishAuthoritativeBalance`) and to the warm-cache republish
(`republishOrgBalanceHint`), issuing **zero** `getOrganizationBalanceSnapshot`
calls on the settlement path.

Because the publication is now pinned to this debit's own committed transaction
result, a **concurrent newer-revision debit** committing during the fenced
handoff can no longer leak into this settlement's authoritative publication —
previously the readback could observe and publish that unrelated newer
balance/revision.

Preserved unchanged: revision-aware admission fencing (`lowerCommittedBalance`
still fires first with the committed revision), collection/payout accounting,
replay handling (idempotent debit key), warm-cache handoff, and the
publication-failure cache cleanup (`invalidateOrgBalanceHint` on the
`error-policy:J2` path).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / performance (removes a redundant SQL read on a hot settlement path)
plus a correctness hardening (revision pinning).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. No public API, schema, or behavior contract changes; only the internal
settlement readback is removed.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/__tests__/affiliate-payout-outbox.test.ts`
— the real in-memory PGlite settlement suite, now seeded with the real
`0177_organization_balance_revision.sql` trigger so committed `balance_revision`
values actually advance on each debit.

- **fenced affiliate settlement publishes its committed debit result without a balance readback** — pauses on the fence publication (not the removed readback), asserts the warm hint shows the committed lower balance mid-flight, asserts the published balance/revision equal the transaction's committed values, and asserts `getOrganizationBalanceSnapshot` is called **0** times across the whole settlement.
- **concurrent newer-revision debit cannot leak into a fenced affiliate publication** — commits an unrelated newer-revision debit while the publication is paused and proves the fence still receives this debit's own committed balance/revision, never the concurrent `0.03`/newer revision, with **0** snapshot reads.
- Existing initial-debit, replay, insufficient/uncollected-overage collection, and alarm/late-settlement race tests remain and pass.

# Risks

It corrects previously-inverted per-sender chronology metadata; it does not alter the HTTP/mailto unsubscribe flow, the SSRF guard, the authorization gate, persistence schema, or any public type. # Background

## What does this PR do?

`InboxUnsubscribeService.scanEmailSubscriptions` aggregated per-sender metadata assuming Gmail search results arrive **oldest-first**. They do not. The Gmail seam (`google-gmail-seam.ts` → `plugin-google-workspace` `GoogleGmailClient.searchMessages` → `gmail.users.messages.list`) returns messages **newest-first** and passes that order through without re-sorting.

The old loop seeded `firstSeenAt` / `latestSeenAt` / `latestMessageId` / `latestThreadId` from the first message per sender, then on **every** subsequent message overwrote `latestSeenAt` / `latestMessageId` / `latestThreadId` unconditionally. With newest-first input this inverts chronology:

- `firstSeenAt` becomes the **newest** message's time,
- `latestSeenAt` becomes the **oldest**,
- `latestMessageId` / `latestThreadId` point at the **oldest** message.

That inverted data flows into the scan feed (`EmailSubscriptionScanResult`) and the persisted `EmailUnsubscribeRecord.metadata.latestMessageId/latestThreadId`, so any UI or downstream logic reading "first seen", "latest seen", or the latest-message pointer was wrong.

**Fix (one function, ~15 lines):** aggregate by timestamp instead of arrival order. Extend `firstSeenAt` downward (min) and `latestSeenAt` upward (max) by `Date.parse` comparison, and only re-point `latestMessageId`/`latestThreadId` when the message is at least as new as the current latest. Unparseable `receivedAt` values leave the extremes and pointers unchanged, and a missing/invalid seeded extreme is recovered on the next parseable message. This makes the aggregation order-independent and respects the upstream newest-first contract of `gmail.ts` `searchMessages`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The behavior of the documented scan result is corrected to match its type contract (`firstSeenAt`/`latestSeenAt`/`latestMessageId`), not redefined.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Reproduce with the single command the issue cites, from `plugins/plugin-inbox`:

```bash
node ../../node_modules/.bin/vitest run --config ./vitest.config.ts \
  test/unsubscribe-scan-order.probe.test.ts test/unsubscribe-service.test.ts
```

`test/unsubscribe-scan-order.probe.test.ts` is the deterministic reproduction from the issue: a mock `InboxGmailGateway` returns three messages from `news@brand.com` in Gmail's newest-first order (`m3` @ 2026-06-17, `m2` @ 2026-06-16, `m1` @ 2026-06-15). Before the fix it fails at the `firstSeenAt` assertion (`expected '2026-06-17T09:00:00.000Z' to be '2026-06-15T09:00:00.000Z'`); after the fix it passes.

## Detailed testing steps

Three permanent regression cases were added to `test/unsubscribe-service.test.ts`:

1. **newest-first feed** → asserts `firstSeenAt`=oldest, `latestSeenAt`=newest, `latestMessageId`/`latestThreadId`=newest.
2. **shuffled order** (newest message in the middle) → asserts the same extremes, proving order-independence.
3. **one unparseable `receivedAt`** → asserts the valid extremes still win and the bad message never hijacks the latest-message pointer.

## The one-function diff

<details>
<summary><b>src/inbox/unsubscribe-service.ts</b></summary>

```diff
       existing.messageCount += 1;
-      existing.latestSeenAt = message.receivedAt;
-      existing.latestMessageId = message.id;
-      existing.latestThreadId = message.threadId;
       existing.allMessageIds.push(message.id);
       existing.allThreadIds.push(message.threadId);
+      // Gmail's `users.messages.list` (consumed via the Gmail seam without
+      // re-sorting) returns newest-first, so iteration order is not chronology.
+      // Derive the extremes by timestamp instead of assuming arrival order:
+      // extend firstSeenAt downward, latestSeenAt upward, and only re-point the
+      // latest-message pointers when this message is at least as new as the
+      // current latest. Unparseable timestamps leave the extremes unchanged.
+      const receivedAtMs = Date.parse(message.receivedAt);
+      if (Number.isFinite(receivedAtMs)) {
+        const firstSeenMs = Date.parse(existing.firstSeenAt);
+        if (!Number.isFinite(firstSeenMs) || receivedAtMs < firstSeenMs) {
+          existing.firstSeenAt = message.receivedAt;
+        }
+        const latestSeenMs = Date.parse(existing.latestSeenAt);
+        if (!Number.isFinite(latestSeenMs) || receivedAtMs >= latestSeenMs) {
+          existing.latestSeenAt = message.receivedAt;
+          existing.latestMessageId = message.id;
+          existing.latestThreadId = message.threadId;
+        }
+      }
```

</details>

## Upstream ordering contract

`plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/gmail.ts` `searchMessages` calls `gmail.users.messages.list({ userId: "me", q, maxResults })` and returns the results in the Gmail API's default order (newest-first) with no ascending re-sort. `google-gmail-seam.ts` `searchGmail` maps that order straight through. The aggregation must therefore treat iteration order as unordered w.r.t. time — which this fix does.

70:T17eb,# What

`agent-backup-restore-coordinator-runtime.ts` opens with a promise:

> Reading configuration while disabled is itself a hazard… a dependent flag set without its parent is an **error rather than a no-op**.

Both flags were then tested with `=== "1"`, so every value that is not `"1"` read as unset — including values that plainly mean *on*. I probed the real exported function before writing anything:

| environment | before | documented intent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `FAILOVER_ENABLED=1` alone | throws "requires …COORDINATOR_ENABLED=1" | ✅ |
| `FAILOVER_ENABLED=true` alone | **`{enabled:false}`** | should refuse |
| `COORDINATOR_ENABLED=1` + `FAILOVER_ENABLED=1` | throws "not implemented" | ✅ |
| `COORDINATOR_ENABLED=1` + `FAILOVER_ENABLED=true` | **coordinator running, `automaticFailoverEnabled:false`** | should refuse |
| `COORDINATOR_ENABLED=true` | **`{enabled:false}`** | should refuse |

The middle row is the one that bothers me: an operator asks for a failover feature this file says is **not implemented**, and gets a running coordinator with failover silently off instead of the loud refusal the code was written to give.

Both guarantees in the header are defeated by a single-character difference in an env var, and the file is inconsistent with itself about it — `readBoundedInteger`, ten lines away, rejects `"60_000"` and `"0"` loudly rather than falling back to a default.

# The change

Parse the flags as strictly as the integer tunables beside them:

```ts
function readStrictFlag(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv, name: string): boolean {
  const raw = env[name];
  if (raw === undefined || raw === "" || raw === "0") return false;
  if (raw !== "1") {
    throw new Error(`${name} must be "1" or "0" when set, or left unset`);
  }
  return true;
}
```

**`"0"` is accepted as off on purpose.** My first version rejected everything but `"1"` and unset, which made `COORDINATOR_ENABLED=0` throw — trading this bug for a worse one, since `0` is unambiguous intent and refusing it turns a deployment that means "off" into a boot failure. Only genuinely ambiguous values are refused now, and the comment says why so nobody tightens it later.

Both existing refusal tests use `"1"` and are untouched: behavior for well-formed values is identical.

# Mutation results

Each new case pins one direction, and neither is redundant:

| mutation | result |
| --- | --- |
| revert to the lenient `raw === "1"` | **6 pass / 1 fail** — only `refuses a flag value that means on but is not "1"` |
| re-apply my over-strict first version (reject `"0"` too) | **6 pass / 1 fail** — only `accepts "0" and empty as off` |
| unmodified | 7 pass / 0 fail |

# Blast radius

A repo-wide grep finds **no** workflow, env template, script, or config that sets either variable, so no existing deployment configuration changes behavior. The coordinator is off by default and its consumer isn't wired yet, which makes this the cheap moment to fix the parse rather than after something depends on it.

# How I got here, and what I deliberately left alone

A file-level sweep for modules imported only by tests (the one that produced #30504 and #30505) flagged this module along with the other three `agent-backup-restore-v3-*` files, `shared-memory-dedicated-export.ts`, and `r2-storage-adapter.ts`.

**Those are not orphans and I'm not proposing anything about them.** Their docstrings describe a deliberately staged, disabled-first feature — *"owns neither copy selection nor failover"*, *"stops after a digest-pinned Docker container is created in the stopped, networkless quarantine"*, *"an off-by-default feature"*. That's ~3,200 lines of work-in-progress that is correctly unwired, and it reads as intentional. The only thing I'm changing is a parsing bug inside the gate itself.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`sanitizeTrajectoryJsonValueInternal` rebuilt both the plain-object and `Map`
branches with `output[key] = sanitized` on a `{}` accumulator. `JSON.parse`
creates `__proto__` as an **own data property**, so `Object.entries` surfaces it,
the loop sanitizes it — and then the assignment invokes the `__proto__` *setter*
instead of defining an own entry.

Two things go wrong at once:

```
input own keys : [ 'path', '__proto__' ]
input JSON     : {"path":"/etc/passwd","__proto__":{"approved":true}}
output own keys: [ 'path' ]
output JSON    : {"path":"/etc/passwd"}
prototype replaced?: true
inherited leak -> out.approved: true
```

The key is **silently dropped** from the recorded value, and the sanitized
object's prototype is **replaced by the caller's own data**, so `out.approved`
reads `true` on a value the sanitizer had just declared safe. (Global
`Object.prototype` is not affected — the pollution is per-returned-object.)

This runs over model-generated planner tool arguments.
`boundStepsForPersistence` in `features/trajectories/TrajectoriesService.ts`
sanitizes `step.action`, `step.observation`, every `step.llmCalls` entry and
`step.metadata` before `stringifyTrajectoryJsonForSql` writes the row, and
`runtime/trajectory-recorder.ts` records tool stage I/O on the planner path. So a
persisted trajectory could misrepresent the arguments the model actually emitted
— including in the training-data export surface.

The fix stages both accumulators on `Object.create(null)`, defines own
properties, and restores `Object.prototype` before returning. That is the
pattern already used by `defineOwn` in `database/connector-json.ts` and the
staging object in `utils/well-formed.ts`, which solved this same class in #18081.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`defineOwnEntry` and the two accumulators in
`packages/core/src/services/trajectory-json.ts`, then the
`trajectory JSON own-key preservation` block appended to
`packages/core/src/services/trajectory-json.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run --cwd packages/core test -- src/services/trajectory-json.test.ts
```

Four new cases through the exported `sanitizeTrajectoryJsonValue` /
`sanitizeTrajectoryJsonObject`: an own `__proto__` key is preserved rather than
dropped; the returned object is not re-parented and exposes no inherited member;
the same holds nested inside a recorded object; and the same holds for an entry
recorded through a `Map`. A fifth pins that ordinary records are unchanged.

```
 × keeps an own __proto__ key instead of dropping it
 × does not re-parent the sanitized object to caller data
 × preserves an own __proto__ key nested inside a recorded object
 × keeps an own __proto__ entry recorded through a Map
 Tests  4 failed | 2 passed (6)
```

With the fix, `Tests 6 passed (6)`.

# Risks

Low. One data file and one new test. Every changed catalog value is replaced by the exact `defaultValue` its own call site already passes, so the English text becomes what the component author wrote rather than anything new; no key is added or removed (`check-i18n.mjs` stays green: 4,438 literal keys, 8,726 source keys × 8 locales). Three of the replacements introduce a placeholder the catalog previously lacked (`{{mode}}`, `{{name}}`, `{{source}}`); each call site already supplies that variable, and other locales may drop a placeholder the source has, so no translation is invalidated. The 21 key-named entries whose call site default is a *different* label ("Secrets" vs "Vault") are deliberately untouched and listed in the issue for a product decision.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`t()` resolves `localized[key] ?? english[key] ?? defaultValue` (`packages/ui/src/i18n/index.ts:48`), so the catalog always wins over the call site. Thirty-seven `en.json` entries held the key's own Title-Cased name as their value — the backfill artifact "Unsaved Changes Title" for `charactereditor.UnsavedChangesTitle` — and shipped verbatim: the character editor's unsaved-changes dialog was titled "Unsaved Changes Title" with the body "Unsaved Changes Body", its counts read "3 Conversation Count", the computer-use overlay was titled "Title" with the body "Body" and the mode line "Mode Line", and a button read "Dont Save".

This replaces those 37 values with the text each call site intends and adds `en-catalog-stubs.test.ts`, which (a) pins every corrected entry through the real `createTranslator("en")` and (b) scans every literal `t("key", { defaultValue })` call site in tracked `packages/ui/src` sources so a key cannot be catalogued as its own name again while its call site supplies real text. The scan flags two shapes only — a multi-word label degraded to Title Case, or a key name standing in for a sentence or placeholder-bearing string — and carries a reasoned allowlist (one entry: `common.loading`, whose call sites disagree on a trailing ellipsis).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Provider and Model Disclosure

# Risks

Low. Two `throw` sites in `fetchWithSsrfGuard` change class from `Error` to `SsrfBlockedError` (a subclass of `Error`, same messages). Consumers that already branch on `SsrfBlockedError` (`ManagedProviderHttpClient`, the Google Maps adapter) start classifying these as blocked instead of retryable; consumers that match on the message or catch `Error` are unaffected. No other package matches these messages against the core guard (the orchestrator's `safeFetch` and cloud `safe-fetch` are separate implementations).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`fetchWithSsrfGuard` in `packages/core/src/network/fetch-guard.ts` threw a plain `Error` for its two redirect-policy violations, `Too many redirects (limit: N)` and `Redirect loop detected`. `ManagedProviderHttpClient` maps `SsrfBlockedError` to `ENDPOINT_BLOCKED` (non-retryable) and every other failure to `PROVIDER_NETWORK` (retryable), and the Maps adapter does the same with `MAPS_ENDPOINT_BLOCKED`, so a provider that answered a `maxRedirects: 0` request with a 302 was retried even though the same request can never succeed. Both violations are deterministic network-policy blocks and now throw `SsrfBlockedError` with the same messages. The missing-`Location` case stays a plain error: it is a malformed response, not a policy decision.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking for message-based consumers; a deliberate classification change for the two consumers that branch on the error class, which is the point of the fix).

# Documentation changes needed?

None.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The two throw sites in `packages/core/src/network/fetch-guard.ts`, the two new cases in `fetch-guard.test.ts`, and the updated zero-hop-budget case in `managed-provider/http-client.test.ts` (it previously pinned `PROVIDER_NETWORK` for a redirect and now pins `ENDPOINT_BLOCKED` with an `SsrfBlockedError` cause).
